Filtrations on equivariant quantum cohomology and Hilbert-Poincaré series
Alexander F. Ritter, Filip Živanović

Abstract
. We prove that Floer theory induces a filtration by ideals on equivariant quantum cohomology of symplectic manifolds equipped with a ℂ ∗ \mathbb{C}^{*} -action. In particular, this gives rise to Hilbert-Poincaré polynomials on ordinary cohomology that depend on Floer theory. En route, the paper develops structural properties of filtrations on three versions of equivariant Floer cohomology. We obtain an explicit presentation for equivariant symplectic cohomology in the Calabi-Yau and Fano settings.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Motivation

The goal of this paper is to study structural properties of equivariant quantum cohomology for non-compact symplectic manifolds
(Y,\omega)
admitting Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-actions
\varphi
.
This will involve foundational work on equivariant Hamiltonian Floer cohomology.
In order to make sense of Floer theory, we make some further assumptions. We assume that
\varphi
extends to a pseudoholomorphic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action for some
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure
I
, because the majority of interesting examples arise in this way, from Algebraic Geometry and Geometric Representation Theory.
1
1

1

Nakajima quiver varieties;
moduli spaces of Higgs bundles;
Kähler quotients of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
including semiprojective
toric manifolds;
hypertoric varieties;
cotangent bundles of flag varieties;
negative complex vector bundles;
crepant resolutions of quotient singularities
\mathbb{C}^{n}/G
for finite subgroups
G\subset SL(n,\mathbb{C})
; all Conical Symplectic Resolutions; and most
equivariant resolutions of affine singularities.

Unfortunately, the past Symplectic Topology literature only dealt with non-compact symplectic manifolds that are “convex at infinity”, so symplectomorphic to
(\Sigma\times[R_{0},\infty),d(R\alpha))
at infinity, where
(\Sigma,\alpha)
is a closed contact manifold.
This almost never applies in the above examples.
Instead, the spaces belong to a class we introduced in
[
RŽ23
]
, called symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifolds, whose defining property is the existence of
a proper pseudoholomorphic
2
2

2

On
\Sigma\times[R_{0},\infty)
, a choice of
d(R\alpha)
-compatible almost complex structure of contact type is made.
map

\Psi:Y^{\mathrm{out}}\to\Sigma\times[R_{0},\infty)

defined outside of the interior of a compact set, such that
\Psi
sends the Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-vector field to the Reeb vector field.

For example, Conical Symplectic Resolutions (in particular all quiver varieties) arise in this way and are much studied in Algebraic Geometry. In that case,
c_{1}(Y)=0
, and quantum cohomology equals ordinary cohomology because
Y
can be deformed to an affine variety.
An interesting feature however, is that they are usually not
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-equivariantly deformable to an affine variety, and often their equivariant quantum cohomology is not classical. From this point of view, the equivariant theory for symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifolds is of greater interest than the non-equivariant theory, as it detects non-trivial Gromov-Witten invariants. This was the main motivation for our work. We also highlight recent work by Jae Hee Lee
[
Lee24
]
that investigates the mod
p
equivariant quantum cohomology of these spaces.

For any symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifold, we constructed Hamiltonian Floer cohomology
HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
and symplectic cohomology
SH^{*}(Y)=\varinjlim HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
in
[
RŽ23
]
. Here
\lambda
is a slope parameter that grows to infinity in the limit. Recall that the generators of
HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
are Hamiltonian
1
-orbits of the Hamiltonian
H_{\lambda}:Y\to\mathbb{R}
; these correspond to
S^{1}
-orbits of
\varphi
of various periods. We choose
H_{\lambda}=c(H)
to be a suitable function of the moment map
H
, of slope
c^{\prime}(H)=\lambda
for large
H
, so that the
1
-orbits are of two types: constant
1
-orbits at points of the components
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
of the fixed locus
\mathfrak{F}=Y^{S^{1}}=Y^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}
, and Morse–Bott manifolds
B_{p,\beta}
of non-constant
1
-orbits corresponding to
S^{1}
-orbits of various periods
p=c^{\prime}(H)
. In
[
RŽ24
]
we construct Morse–Bott–Floer spectral sequences that effectively compute
HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
and
SH^{*}(Y)
in terms of the cohomologies of the Morse–Bott manifold
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
and
B_{p,\beta}
.

By
[
RŽ23
]
, quantum cohomology
QH^{*}(Y)
is filtered by graded ideals
\mathscr{F}^{p}
ordered by
p\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
,

(1)

\mathscr{F}^{p}:=\bigcap_{\mathrm{generic}\,\lambda\geq p}\left(\ker c_{\lambda}^{*}:QH^{*}(Y)\to HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\right),\qquad\mathscr{F}^{\infty}:=QH^{*}(Y),

where
c_{\lambda}^{*}
is a Floer continuation map, a grading-preserving
QH^{*}(Y)
-module homomorphism.
That filtration is an invariant of
Y
up to isomorphism of symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifolds (pseudoholomorphic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-equivariant symplectomorphisms, without conditions on the
\Psi
map).
The real parameter
p
is a part of that invariant, and has a geometric interpretation:
x\in\mathscr{F}^{p}
means that
x
can be represented as a Floer chain involving non-constant
S^{1}
-orbits of period
\leq p.

1.2.
Overview of
S^{1}
-equivariant chain models

S^{1}
-equivariant Floer theory involves moduli spaces of maps into the Borel model
S^{\infty}\times_{S^{1}}Y
that can contribute arbitrarily high powers of the equivariant parameter
u\in H^{2}(\mathbb{C}P^{\infty})
in the construction of the Floer chain differential,

d=\delta_{0}+u\delta_{1}+u^{2}\delta_{2}+\cdots,

where
\delta_{i}
does not involve
u
, and
\delta_{0}
is the non-equivariant Floer differential.
For this reason, there are three natural equivariant complexes one can build
3
3

3

[\![u]\!]
means we take a completed tensor product with the
\mathbb{K}
-algebra
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
of power series in
u
; whereas
(\!(u)\!)
means we
u
-localise the
[\![u]\!]
-version, so formal Laurent series in
u
. The symbol
(\cdots)_{u}
always denotes
u
-localisation.
from a non-equivariant Floer chain complex
C^{*}
:

E^{-}C^{*}:=C^{*}[\![u]\!],\quad\;E^{\infty}C^{*}:=C^{*}(\!(u)\!)=(E^{-}C^{*})_{u},\quad\;E^{+}C^{*}:=(E^{-}C^{*})_{u}/uE^{-}C^{*}\cong E^{-}C^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}\mathbb{F},

where
\mathbb{F}:=\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
. Here
\mathbb{K}
is a suitable Novikov field of coefficients, which we assume is of characteristic zero.
The above are all
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules; and
E^{\infty}C^{*}
is also a
\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)
-module.
On cohomology,
(E^{-}H^{*})_{u}\cong E^{\infty}H^{*}
are canonically isomorphic for algebraic reasons (flatness of
u
-localisation).

For small slopes,
\lambda=0^{+}
, using
C^{*}=CF^{*}(H_{0^{+}})
, yields Morse-Bott models for “classical” equivariant cohomology
E^{\diamondsuit}H^{*}(Y)
for each of the three models
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty,+\}
, indeed only finitely many
\delta_{j}
are non-zero and
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{0^{+}})\cong H^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
is the Borel construction of
S^{1}
-equivariant cohomology; the other two models are classically motivated by cyclic homology.

Increasing the slope
\lambda
gives rise to continuation maps:
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module homomorphisms

E^{\diamondsuit}c^{*}_{\lambda}:E^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda}),

defined for generic
\lambda>0
,
abbreviated
c^{*}_{\lambda}
when there is no ambiguity.
E^{\diamondsuit}c^{*}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
factors through
E^{\diamondsuit}c^{*}_{\lambda}
whenever
\lambda<\lambda^{\prime}
, and the direct limit as
\lambda\to\infty
defines

(2)

E^{\diamondsuit}c^{*}:E^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}(Y),

often abbreviated
c^{*}
.
For
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty\}
, the equivariant quantum product on equivariant cohomology can be defined by
parametrising the quantum product via the Borel model (e.g. see
[
LJ20
,
LJ21
]
). For
\diamondsuit=+
, there is no product as there is no multiplication map
\mathbb{F}\times\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}
.
In general, there is no product on
E^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}(Y)
, but we discuss an exception to this in
Section

8
.
We abbreviate
E^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y):=E^{\diamondsuit}H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{K})
, with the convention that for
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty\}
we use the equivariant quantum product.

Example 1.1
.

For the space
M=S^{1}
, with the standard
S^{1}
-rotation action, the classical Morse models
E^{-},E^{\infty},E^{+}
for equivariant cohomology are:
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

where the Morse function has two critical points: a minimum
x
and a maximum
y
, so grading
|x|=0
,
|y|=1
,
|u|=2
.
Thus
E^{-}SH^{*}(M)=\mathbb{K}\cdot x
whereas
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(M)=0
, and
E^{+}SH^{*}(M)=\mathbb{K}\cdot y
(all three are torsion
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules with trivial
u
-action).
This is consistent with Borel’s localisation theorem:

(3)

H^{*}_{S^{1}}(M)_{u}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}(H^{*}_{S^{1}}(\mathrm{Fixed\;locus}))_{u}=H^{*}(\mathrm{Fixed\;locus})(\!(u)\!).

The same holds for the weight
k\notin\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
circle action: the red arrows are multiplication by
k
, so isomorphisms, as
\mathrm{char}\,\mathbb{K}=0
. This is the lowest-order approximation of local Floer cohomology near an isolated
S^{1}
-family of Hamiltonian
1
-orbits. If
\mathrm{char}\,\mathbb{K}\neq 0
, torsion arises and Borel’s theorem fails.
Even the simplest examples display interesting but complicated torsion phenomena, see
[
LJ20
]
. We therefore chose to assume
\text{char}\,\mathbb{K}=0
in order to obtain clean general statements and computations.

Equivariant Floer theory actually involves two
S^{1}
-actions acting on
1
-periodic Hamiltonian orbits
x=x(t):S^{1}\to Y
. The
\varphi
-action
on
Y
, and the
loop-action
that reparametrises the domain of
x
:

(w\cdot x)(t)=\varphi_{w}(x(t)),\quad\textrm{ and }\quad(w\cdot x)(t)=x(t+\theta),

where
w=e^{2\pi i\theta}\in S^{1}\subset\mathbb{C}^{*}.
Abbreviate
\varphi_{e^{2\pi i\theta}}
by
\varphi_{\theta}
, for
\theta\in S^{1}:=[0,1]/(0\sim 1)
, and parametrise loops by
t\in[0,1]/(0\sim 1)
. A
weight

(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
defines an
S^{1}
-action on free loops
x=x(t)\in\mathcal{L}Y
:

(4)

\theta\cdot x:t\mapsto\varphi_{a\theta}(x(t-b\theta)).

Remark 1.2
.

We chose to consider just one mixed
S^{1}
-action
at a time, as in (
4
), yielding one equivariant parameter
u
, rather than the full
S^{1}\times S^{1}
-action which would produce two equivariant parameters.
In
Section

1.8
we comment on what happens when we vary
(a,b).

The
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
(arising, as explained, from small slopes
\lambda>0
), only depends on the
\varphi^{a}
-action on
Y
, not
4
4

4

the loop-action is trivial on constant Hamiltonian
1
-orbits, which are the generators of the Morse-Bott complex.
on
b
. Let
QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)
be equivariant quantum cohomology for the
S^{1}
-action
\varphi^{a}
, working over
\mathbb{K}[u]
-coefficients. For grading reasons, we have a canonical
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-algebra isomorphism

E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\cong QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!].

If
x
is a Hamiltonian
1
-orbit of
H_{\lambda}
, then
x
is a fixed point of (
4
) precisely when
x
lies in a Morse–Bott manifold
B_{p,\beta}
with
p=\tfrac{a}{b}
: the
\varphi
-rotation and loop-action perfectly cancel out.

So we call
(a,b)
a
free weight
if
\frac{a}{b}
is not equal to an
S^{1}
-period
p
for which a Morse–Bott manifold
B_{p,\beta}
exists. We call those “bad”
p
the

non-generic slopes
, and by
[
RŽ23
]
they are precisely:

(5)

\{\tfrac{k}{m}:m\in W^{+},k\geq 1\in\mathbb{Z}\},

where
W^{+}:=\{
strictly positive weights
m
of the fixed components
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}\}
. The discrete subset
W^{+}\subset[0,\infty)
can be easily computed
[
RŽ23
]
.
So:
(a,b)
free
\Leftrightarrow aW^{+}\cap b\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}=\emptyset
.

For example,
(a,b)\neq(0,0)
is free if
a\cdot b\leq 0
. Also
(1,0)
is free, and
(1,0)
is the only weight for which there is a pair-of-pants product on
E^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}(Y)
for
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty\}
, so (
2
) is a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-algebra homomorphism. That product arises as a direct limit of products
E^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda_{1}})\otimes E^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda_{2}})\to E^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}})
.

Remark 1.3

(Zhao’s work)
.

The case
(a,b)=(0,1)
is also free.
This case has been studied extensively in the literature because it only uses the loop-action; no
\varphi
-action is needed.
In particular, Zhao
[
Zha17
]

investigated this case for Liouville manifolds
M
.
We remark how Zhao’s terminology relates to ours: “localised periodic” symplectic cohomology
HP^{*}_{S^{1},\mathrm{loc}}(M)=E^{\infty}SH^{*}(M)
, and “periodic” symplectic cohomology
HP^{*}_{S^{1}}(M)=E^{\infty}_{\textrm{telescope}}SH^{*}(M)
involves a telescope model, see
Section

5.4
.

Zhao proved a localisation theorem in that setting: using
\text{char}\,\mathbb{K}=0
,
the canonical homomorphism
E^{\infty}c^{*}:H^{*}(M)(\!(u)\!)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(M)
is an isomorphism.
One of our main theorems,
Theorem

1.14
, is a general localisation theorem for all symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifolds, for any free weight. We use a different proof than Zhao’s explicit one: we deduce it from Borel’s theorem (
3
) applied to the equivariant analogue of Morse–Bott–Floer spectral sequences, from
[
RŽ24
]
.

1.3.
Equivariant formality and computation of equivariant symplectic cohomology

By adapting a classical result of Kirwan
[
Kir84
, Sec.5.8]
, we prove:

Lemma 1.4

(Equivariant formality)
.

There are non-canonical
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module isomorphisms

E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\cong H^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!],\quad E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\cong H^{*}(Y)(\!(u)\!),\quad E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\cong H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{F}.

Just as in the non-equivariant case, the
c^{*}
map (
2
) can be unpredictable. In the “Calabi-Yau” setting
c_{1}(Y)=0
, we showed
SH^{*}(Y)=0
in
[
RŽ23
]
, which motivated our interest in the filtration (
1
).
In the “monotone” or “Fano” setting
c_{1}(Y)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}[\omega]
, the non-equivariant
c^{*}
map is a quotient map onto

SH^{*}(Y)\cong QH^{*}(Y)/E_{0},\textrm{ where }E_{0}=\ker Q_{\varphi}^{y},

where
y:=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\,H^{*}(Y)
, and
Q_{\varphi}\in QH^{*}(Y)
is a “rotation class”
[
RŽ23
]
acting by quantum multiplication on
QH^{*}(Y)
(a generalisation of the Seidel element for
\varphi

[
Sei97
]
to the non-compact setting
[
Rit14
,
Rit16
]
).
One can also think of
E_{0}
in terms of “positive symplectic cohomology”, arising from the quotient complexes in which only non-constant Hamiltonian
1
-orbits are considered,

E_{0}\cong SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)[-1].

The notation
[-1]
means we shift the grading up by one.
We now summarise the main relations that we prove between the equivariant and non-equivariant theories for any symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifold
Y
.

Lemma 1.5
.

There is a short exact sequence, commuting with the maps from (
2
),

(6)

0\longrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow 0.

There are long exact sequences

(7)

\displaystyle\cdots\longrightarrow SH^{*}(Y)[-1]\longrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\;\;-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{set }u=0\;\;\;}}{{\longrightarrow}}\!\!\!SH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow\cdots

(8)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\cdots\longrightarrow SH^{*}(Y)[-2]\;\;-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u^{0}\textrm{-incl}\;\;\;}}{{\longrightarrow}}\!\!\!E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow SH^{*}(Y)[-1]\longrightarrow\cdots\!\!\!\!

Vanishing criterion:
SH^{*}(Y)=0\Leftrightarrow E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0
, in which case
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y).

Theorem 1.6
.

Let
(a,b)
be any free weight.
Then
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y).

If
c_{1}(Y)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[\omega]
, there is a commutative diagram of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules

\textstyle{E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
injective
\scriptstyle{E^{-}c^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
injective
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\!SH^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]}\!\oplus\!E_{0}[\![u]\!]\!\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subset}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\!SH^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]}\!\oplus\!E_{0}(\!(u)\!)\!\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\!SH^{*}(Y)(\!(u)\!)}\!\oplus\!E_{0}(\!(u)\!)\!}

and the cokernel of the horizontal maps on the right is:

E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbf{SH^{*}(Y)}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{F}.

The vertical arrows are non-canonical isomorphisms, however the copy of
E_{0}(\!(u)\!)
inside
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is canonical: it is the largest
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodule on which multiplication by
u
acts as an invertible map.

If
c_{1}(Y)=0
, the above simplifies to:

E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)

and

E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0.

Remark 1.7

(Further explanations)
.

Using
Lemma

1.4
, there is an injection

QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
, whose image freely generates
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
as a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module.
The inverse of the first vertical arrow in the theorem arises by identifying
SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathrm{image}(Q_{\varphi}^{y})\subset QH^{*}(Y)
and
E_{0}=\ker Q_{\varphi}^{y}\subset QH^{*}(Y)
, and then uses that injection combined with the free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-action.

The map
E^{-}c^{*}
equals the natural
u
-localisation map but only
u
-localising the submodule
E_{0}[\![u]\!]
.
The second horizontal map is
u
times the canonical
u
-localisation map,
u
-localising everything.

The middle vertical arrow can be thought of as a short exact sequence of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules,

0\to E_{0}(\!(u)\!)\to E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\to SH^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]\to 0.

By splitting, so by picking a lift of a basis of the free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
SH^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]
, we see there are many copies of
SH^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]
inside
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
, even though in the proof we construct a specific copy. By contrast, the copy of
E_{0}(\!(u)\!)
is canonical, and we build generators by adding very specific higher order
u
-corrections to a
\mathbb{K}
-linear basis of
E_{0}\subset QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
.

Example 1.8
.

For
Y=\mathbb{C}^{n}
, we have
QH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\mathbb{K}
,
SH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})=0
.
In this case we could simply use
\mathbb{K}=\mathbb{Q}(\!(T)\!)
, with grading
|T|=0
.
For the standard
S^{1}
-action, and free
(a,b)
(equivalently,
a\notin b\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
):

QH^{*}_{S^{1}}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\mathbb{K}[u],\;\ E^{-}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!],\;\ E^{-}SH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!),\;\ E^{+}SH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})=0.

Example 1.9
.

The total space
Y
of the negative line bundle
\pi:\mathcal{O}(-k)\to\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{m}
for
1\leq k\leq m
is monotone,
c_{1}(Y)=(1+m-k)x\neq 0
where
x:=\pi^{*}[\omega_{\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{m}}]
. To simplify notation, in this case we can use
\mathbb{K}=\mathbb{Q}(\!(T)\!),
with grading

|T|=2
.
Abbreviate

y:=x^{1+m-k}-(-k)^{k}T^{1+m-k}
.
By
[
Rit16
, Cor.4.14]
,

QH^{*}(Y)=\mathbb{K}[x]/(x^{k}y)\cong\mathbb{K}^{1+m},\quad Q_{\varphi}=-kx,\quad E_{0}=\langle y\rangle\cong\mathbb{K}^{k},\quad SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}[x]/(y)\cong\mathbb{K}^{1+m-k},

where
\varphi
is the standard
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on fibres.
Therefore, for free weights
(a,b)
(equivalently
a\notin b\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
),

\begin{split}QH^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}[u]^{1+m},\;E^{-}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{1+m},\;E^{\infty}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{1+m},\;E^{+}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\mathbb{F}^{1+m},\\
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{1+m-k}\oplus\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{k},\qquad E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{1+m},\qquad E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=\mathbb{F}^{1+m-k}.\quad\end{split}

Abbreviate:

s=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}SH^{*}(Y),\qquad e=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}E_{0},\qquad y=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}H^{*}(Y)=s+e.

Theorem 1.10
.

Assume
c_{1}(Y)\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[\omega]
.
For any weight
(a,b)\neq(0,0)
, whether free or not, we have:

E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{s}\oplus\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{e},\qquad E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{y},\qquad E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{F}^{s},

which simplifies when
c_{1}(Y)=0
:

E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{y}\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
and
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0
.

In the free weight case, this follows from
Theorem

1.6
, moreover
c^{*}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
corresponds to the inclusion
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{s}\oplus\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{e}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{s}\oplus\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{e}
. The non-free weight case satisfies the same isomorphisms, but the latter
c^{*}
map can have kernel as
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{e}\to\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{e}
may no longer be an inclusion. To avoid complicating the introduction, we do not explain the general result further (see
Theorem

14.3
and
Section

7.4
), and instead we describe the simplest example.

Example 1.11

(Non-free weights)
.

In
Example

1.8
, consider the non-free weight
(a,b)=(1,1)
. Each point of
S^{2n-1}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
is the initial point of a great circle, which are orbits of the
S^{1}
-action. This corresponds to a Morse–Bott manifold
B_{1}\cong S^{2n-1}
of
1
-orbits for slope value
1
, which are all fixed points of (
4
). The maximum of an auxiliary Morse function on
B_{1}
will kill the unit
1\in H^{0}(Y)
in the equivariant spectral sequence for
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
: indeed, that edge differential exists already in the non-equivariant spectral sequence for
SH^{*}(Y)
. So
E^{-}c^{*}_{1^{+}}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{1^{+}})
and
E^{\infty}c^{*}_{1^{+}}:E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{1^{+}})
both vanish (in stark contrast to the free weight case, where such maps are injective).
However, the minimum of the auxiliary Morse function on
B_{1}
also creates a new
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module generator in degree
-2n
, indeed

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{1^{+}})\cong E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)[2n]
.
The continuation maps
E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{1^{+}})\to E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
all have to be injective for generic slopes
\lambda>1
(for general reasons, by
Theorem

6.21
). So
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)[2n]\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)
even though the map
E^{\infty}c^{*}
in (
2
) is zero! From there it is not too difficult to see that
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
and
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0.

1.4.
Localisation theorem, injectivity and torsion-freeness

Beyond those descriptions of equivariant cohomology, we are interested in how equivariant Floer theory induces filtrations on ordinary and quantum cohomology.
Recall we considered (
1
) due to the phenomenon that kernels of continuation maps grow in an interesting way.
The same phenomenon occurs in the
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
theory, by considering the kernels
E_{1}^{\lambda}:=\ker(E^{+}c_{\lambda}^{*}:\;E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda}))
. Indeed there is more structure:
for
j\in\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\pm\infty\}
,
p\in[0,\infty]
, we define
\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}:=\bigcap_{\mathrm{generic}\,\lambda\geq p}E_{j}^{\lambda}
where

(9)

E_{j\leq 0}^{\lambda}:=\ker(u^{|j|+1}\cdot E^{+}c_{\lambda}^{*}),\qquad E_{j>0}^{\lambda}:=u^{j-1}\ker E^{+}c_{\lambda}^{*},\qquad E_{-\infty}^{\lambda}:=E^{+}QH^{*}(Y),\;E_{\infty}^{\lambda}:=\{0\}.

The
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodules
\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}\subset E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)
satisfy

\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p^{\prime}}\;\textrm{ for }\;p<p^{\prime},\qquad u\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{E}_{j+1}^{p}\subset\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p},\qquad u\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}=\mathscr{E}_{j+1}^{p}.

(The second equation is a general property of all filtrations that we will define, whereas the third equation is a special strengthening of the first inclusion of the second equation).

Remark 1.12

(Persistence module and barcode)
.

Just as in the non-equivariant discussion in
[
RŽ23
, Sec.1.6]
, we can build a persistence module
(V_{p})_{p\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}}
with
V_{\lambda}:=E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
for generic
\lambda
, with
V_{\infty}=E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
, with
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}=\ker E^{+}c^{*}_{p^{+}}
playing the role that
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\varphi}
was playing in
[
RŽ23
, Sec.1.6]
. In particular, this assigns a barcode to the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action
\varphi
, and one can use the bottleneck distance to compare barcodes of different
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-actions.
For weights
(a,0)
, the persistence module has the same periodicity property mentioned in
[
RŽ23
, Sec.1.6]
, in view of
Corollary

1.36
.

We saw above that often
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0
, and then the above
\mathscr{E}
-kernels encode very interesting and complicated structural properties of moduli spaces of families of Floer solutions.
What caught us by surprise, is that the precise opposite phenomenon occurs for the kernels when
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty\}
:

Proposition 1.13
.

If the
u
-localisation of
c^{*}\!:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is an isomorphism, then the continuation maps
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
and
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
are injective.

That this occurs rather often, is due to the following, which is at the heart of our paper:

Theorem 1.14

(Localisation theorem)
.

Let
(a,b)
be a free weight. The following diagram of isomorphisms commutes, where the top row is the
u
-localisation of
E^{-}c^{*}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)_{u}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)_{u}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(E^{-}c^{*})_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}c^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

1.5.
Young diagrams and Hilbert-Poincaré series

Proposition

1.13
means that the equivariant analogue of (
1
) for
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty\}
would be trivial. So we seek instead a filtration arising from injective homomorphisms.
The choice in
Remark

1.2
ensures the basic but consequential fact that
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
is a discrete valuation ring. Therefore, any
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module homomorphism
Q:V\to W
of free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules of finite rank admits a Smith normal form
5
5

5

\mathrm{diag}
refers to the main diagonal of a possibly non-square matrix. So
a=\min(\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}V,\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}W)
.

\mathrm{diag}(u^{j_{1}},\ldots,u^{j_{a}})
where
0\leq j_{1}\leq\cdots\leq j_{a}\leq\infty
, abusively allowing the formal symbol
u^{\infty}:=0
(this does not arise when
Q
is injective). We call
u^{j}
the
invariant factors
of
Q
.
Consider first slopes just above an integer, so
\lambda=k^{+}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
(the Hamiltonian flow for slope
k
would give rise to
k
“full rotations” of the
S^{1}
-action, at infinity).

Lemma 1.15
.

The
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is free, indeed it is a copy of
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
shifted in grading.
Thus, we obtain invariant factors
u^{j_{i}(k)}
associated to the homomorphism

(10)

c^{*}_{k^{+}}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}}).

These
j-indices
cannot decrease in
k
, and they satisfy
0\leq j_{1}(k)\leq\ldots\leq j_{y}(k)\leq\infty
,
y=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{K}}H^{*}(Y).

To an injective homomorphism
Q:V\to W
of free finite rank
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules, we associate two polynomials which we call
filtration polynomial

f_{Q}(t)
and
slice polynomial

s_{Q}(t)
:

(11)

\displaystyle f_{Q}=f_{0}+f_{1}t+\cdots+f_{j}t^{j}+\cdots\;\textrm{ where }\;f_{j}=\#\{\textrm{invariant factors }u^{j}\}=\#\{i:j_{i}=j\}.

(12)

\displaystyle s_{Q}=d_{0}+d_{1}t+\cdots+d_{j}t^{j}\!+\cdots\;\textrm{ where }\;d_{j}=\#\{\textrm{invariant factors }u^{\geq j}\}=\#\{i:j_{i}\geq j\}

They are recoverable from each other, using
f_{j}=d_{j}-d_{j+1}
and
d_{0}=\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}\,V.

The derivative
f^{\prime}_{Q}(0)=\sum_{j\geq 1}d_{j}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\textrm{Torsion}(\mathrm{coker}\,Q)
, which becomes
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathrm{coker}\,Q
when
V,W
have equal ranks.

Now view
W
as sitting inside the
u
-localisation
V_{u}
by using
Q_{u}^{-1}=\mathrm{adj}\,Q\otimes\tfrac{1}{\text{det}Q}:W\hookrightarrow V\otimes\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)=V_{u}
.

Example 1.16
.

Q=\mathrm{diag}(u,u^{3},u^{4}):V=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{3}\to\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{3}=W
, so the j-indices are
j_{1}=1
,
j_{2}=3
,
j_{3}=4
. View
W
as the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodule
u^{-1}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus u^{-3}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus u^{-4}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\subset V_{u}=\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{3}
.

f_{Q}=t+t^{3}+t^{4}\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad\;s_{Q}=3+3t+2t^{2}+2t^{3}+t^{4}.

Pictorially,
V=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{3}
is a rectangular shape of vertically stacked dots corresponding to copies of
\mathbb{K}
, with an upward
u
-multiplication map; and
W
“attaches” a Young diagram’s worth of new copies of
\mathbb{K}
:
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
Dual Young diagram: the
[formula]
[formula]
Young diagram for the
[formula]
-slice
[formula]
(French notation: row
lengths non-decreasing)
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

The Young diagram also encodes
f_{j}=\#
(rows of length
j
), so
f_{1}=1,f_{3}=1,f_{4}=1
and
0
otherwise.

As becomes clear from the example,

d_{j}=(u^{-j}\textrm{-slice dimension})=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}(W\cap u^{-j}V)/(W\cap u^{-j+1}V).

Moreover,
W\cap u^{-j}V\cong V\cap u^{j}W
via
w\mapsto u^{j}w
has image

(13)

F_{j}:=V\cap u^{j}W=\{v\in V:Q(v)\textrm{ is }u^{j}\textrm{-divisible in }W\}=\ker(V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Q}}{{\longrightarrow}}W/u^{j}W).

These
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodules
F_{j}\subset V
yield a filtration of
V
, satisfying

(14)

uF_{j}=F_{j+1}\cap uV\subset F_{j+1}\subset F_{j}.

The
u^{-j}
-slice
(W\cap u^{-j}V)/(W\cap u^{-j+1}V)
is canonically
F_{j}/uF_{j-1}
via
w\mapsto u^{j}w.
Abbreviate

(15)

P_{j}:=F_{j}/uF_{j-1}=F_{j}/(F_{j}\cap uV)\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad P:=V/uV,

and note that
P_{j}
filter
P
, with associated graded module
\mathrm{gr}\,P:=\oplus P_{j}/P_{j+1}
. We deduce:

d_{j}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}P_{j}\;\;\textrm{ and }\;\;f_{j}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}P_{j}/P_{j+1}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}F_{j}/(F_{j+1}+uF_{j-1}).

In particular
f_{Q}
, by definition, is the
Hilbert-Poincaré series
of
P
for the filtration
P_{j}
.

Example 1.17
.

In
Example

1.16
, we have:

Note that for all sufficiently large
j
, we have
F_{j}=u^{j}W\subset V
(see the
j=5
case in the picture).

In our setting, (
10
) plays the role of
Q
, so
V=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
. We have a canonical isomorphism
V/uV\cong QH^{*}(Y)
to non-equivariant quantum cohomology. So
f_{Q}
is a Hilbert-Poincaré series for classical cohomology, for a filtration that depends on equivariant quantum and Floer theory.

For general slopes,
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
may not be free, unlike the
\lambda=k^{+}
case. It is however finitely generated over the PID
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
, so the quotient by the torsion submodule is free.
For
W
finitely generated, we can therefore apply the previous setup to
Q:V\to W/T
, where
T=\mathrm{Torsion}(W)
. Thus,

F_{j}:=\{v\in V:Q(v)\textrm{ is }u^{j}\textrm{-divisible in }W/T\}=\ker(V\to W/(T+u^{j}W))

satisfies (
14
), and we leave definition (
15
) unchanged.
For
Q:V\to W
injective, the map
Q:V\to W/T
may have non-trivial kernel
Q^{-1}(T)\subset F_{j}
. This will not happen in our setting, when
Q
is the map
c_{\lambda}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
. Indeed, for
k>\lambda
,
Q
composed with the continuation
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
equals (
10
), which is injective.

1.6.
Filtrations in equivariant Floer theory

We now summarise the outcomes in Floer theory.
Abbreviate
E^{-}_{\lambda}:=E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
with its torsion
T_{\lambda},
and
E^{-}:=\varinjlim E^{-}_{\lambda}=E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
with torsion
T
.

Definition 1.18
.

For any
p\in[0,\infty)
, define the
\mathbf{E^{-}}
-filtration

(16)

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}:=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\bigcap_{\textrm{generic }\lambda\geq p}\!\!\!\!\!\!\ker\left(E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c_{\lambda}^{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{-}_{\lambda}/(T_{\lambda}+u^{j}E^{-}_{\lambda})\right)\quad(\textrm{so }\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\lambda}=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y),\;\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{\lambda}=\ker c_{\lambda}^{*}),

for
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}:=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
for
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{<0}
.
For generic
p
, we can ignore the intersection and let
\lambda=p
.
Also,
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}=\ker c_{p^{+}}^{*}
for all
p^{+}>p
sufficiently close to
p
(such
p^{+}
are generic).
For
p=\infty
, we use
E^{-}:=E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
and
c^{*}=\varinjlim c_{\lambda}^{*}
, so
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\infty}:=\ker(c^{*}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}/(T+u^{j}E^{-}))
.

Proposition 1.19
.

For the free weight
(a,b)=(1,0)
, the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodules
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}\subset E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
are ideals with respect to the equivariant quantum product.

Corollary 1.20
.

The
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodules
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}\subset E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
satisfy

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p^{\prime}}\textrm{ for }p<p^{\prime},\qquad u\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p},\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad u\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}=\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}\cap u\,E^{-}QH^{*}(Y).

Remark 1.21
.

\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}
corresponds to a naive inclusion of
\mathbb{K}
-summands in pictures like those from
Example

1.17
;
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p^{\prime}}
on the other hand is highly non-trivial and is often not the naive inclusion of such pictures. The pictures arise from two steps: first, a map of type
Q_{u}^{-1}:W/T\hookrightarrow V_{u}
is applied, and then a basis of the free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
Q_{u}^{-1}(W/T)\subset V_{u}
needs to be chosen. This second step is typically not compatible with varying
p
, because the basis of
V
needed to put
Q:V\to W/T
into Smith normal form (for a suitable basis of
W/T
) typically depends on
p
.

Definition 1.22
.

For
p\in[0,\infty]
, the
slice dimensions
,
slice series
, and
filtration polynomial
are

d_{j}^{p}:=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}/u\mathscr{F}_{j-1}^{p},\qquad s_{p}(t):=\sum_{j\geq 0}d_{j}^{p}t^{j}\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad f_{p}(t):=\sum_{j\geq 0}f_{j}^{p}t^{j}=\sum_{j\geq 0}(d_{j}^{p}-d_{j+1}^{p})t^{j}.

Corollary 1.23
.

The
d_{j}^{p}
and
f_{j}^{p}
are finite invariants, and they satisfy the following properties:

\displaystyle 0\leq d_{j}^{p}\leq y,\qquad\quad d_{j}^{p}\leq d_{j}^{p^{\prime}}\;

\displaystyle\textrm{ for }\;p\leq p^{\prime},\qquad\quad d_{j}^{p}\leq d_{j+1}^{p},

\displaystyle f_{j}^{p}=\#\{\textrm{invariant factors }u^{j}\textrm{ of }c_{p^{+}}\},\qquad

\displaystyle\textrm{ and }\qquad d_{j}^{p}=\#\{\textrm{invariant factors }u^{\geq j}\textrm{ of }c_{p^{+}}\}

where
y=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}H^{*}(Y)=\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
, and
the invariant factors refer to the Smith normal form of the map

c_{p^{+}}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}_{p^{+}}/T_{p^{+}}.

In particular,
f_{Q}
is always a polynomial, and
s_{Q}
is a polynomial precisely when
c_{p^{+}}
is injective (which holds when
(a,b)
is free, by
Section

1.4
).

Definition 1.24
.

Abbreviate
\mathscr{P}:=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)/uE^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
. This is filtered, for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, by

\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}:=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}/u\mathscr{F}_{j-1}^{p}=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}/(\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}\cap u\,E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)),\qquad\mathscr{P}_{\infty}^{p}:=0,\qquad(\textrm{so }\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}=\mathscr{P}\textrm{ for }j\leq 0),

satisfying
\mathscr{P}_{j+1}^{p}\subset\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}
and
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p^{\prime}}
for
p\leq p^{\prime}.

Define
\mathrm{gr}^{p}_{j}\mathscr{P}:=\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}/\mathscr{P}_{j+1}^{p}
and
\mathrm{gr}^{p}\,\mathscr{P}:=\oplus_{j\geq 0}\mathrm{gr}^{p}_{j}\mathscr{P}
.

Corollary 1.25
.

There is a canonical identification

\mathscr{P}\cong QH^{*}(Y)
with non-equivariant quantum cohomology. Thus quantum cohomology is filtered by
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}
and its Hilbert-Poincaré series equals
f_{p}(t)
:

f_{j}^{p}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}/\mathscr{P}_{j+1}^{p}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathrm{gr}_{j}^{p}\mathscr{P}.

When
(a,b)
is not free,
c_{p^{+}}
may be non-injective:
s_{p}:=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\ker c_{p^{+}}>0
contributes to each
d_{j}^{p}
, and
d_{j}^{p}=s
for all large
j
. One would then allow infinite size Young diagrams in the following definition.

Definition 1.26
.

Let
j_{1}^{p},j_{2}^{p},\ldots
be the distinct exponents that arise in the invariant factors
u^{j}
of
c_{p^{+}}
. The
Young diagram

\mathcal{Y}_{p}
is the Young diagram of shape
(j_{1}^{p},\ldots,j_{1}^{p},j_{2}^{p},\ldots)
, where each
j_{i}^{p}
is repeated
f_{j_{i}}^{p}
times.
Switching rows and columns, we obtain the
dual Young diagram

\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}
, and it is the Young diagram of shape
(d_{1}^{p},d_{2}^{p},\ldots)
in French notation (non-increasing row-lengths
d_{j}^{p}
as in
Example

1.16
).

To
\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}
we associate
\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(\mathscr{P}):=(\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}_{0}\supset\mathscr{P}_{1}^{p}\supset\mathscr{P}_{2}^{p}\supset\cdots)
, which is a filtration of
\mathscr{P}
by
\mathbb{K}
-linear subspaces. To
\mathcal{Y}_{p}
we associate the associated graded
\mathbb{K}
-vector space
\mathcal{Y}_{p}(\mathscr{P}):=\mathrm{gr}^{p}\mathscr{P}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{p}\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{1}^{p}\oplus\cdots
.

Corollary 1.27
.

For
i,j\geq 0
,
p\in[0,\infty]
, we have associations:

\displaystyle(i\textrm{-th row in }\mathcal{Y}_{p}\textrm{ of length }j_{i})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{Y}_{p}(i):=\mathrm{gr}_{j_{i}}^{p}\mathscr{P}\subset\mathrm{gr}^{p}\mathscr{P}\;\textrm{ of dimension}=f_{j_{i}}^{p}=\#(\textrm{rows of length }j_{i}),

\displaystyle(j\textrm{-th row in }\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}\textrm{ of length }d_{j}^{p})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(j):=\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{P}\;\textrm{ of dimension}=d_{j}^{p}.

Vertical upward inclusion of rows in
\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}
corresponds to inclusions
\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(j)=\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}\supset\mathscr{P}_{j+1}^{p}=\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(j+1)
.

Remark 1.28
.

Choose a basis for each
\mathcal{Y}_{p}(i)
. By successive basis extensions, choose lifted bases for
\cdots\subset\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(j)\subset\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(j-1)\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(0)
. The resulting
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module basis for
V
determines a picture as in
Example

1.16
. In that example, the basis for
\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}(2)
spans
0\oplus\mathbb{K}^{2}\subset\mathbb{K}^{3}
and its product by
u^{-2}
gives a basis for the
u^{-2}
-slice. In general, the boxes of
\mathcal{Y}_{p}^{*}
correspond to a
\mathbb{K}
-linear basis for the complement of
V\subset W
, so that vertical upward inclusion of boxes corresponds to
u
-multiplication on the basis, and rows correspond to slices. However, substantial choices were required to pass from the abstract spaces to the picture, e.g. there is no basis-independent choice of
u^{-j}
-slice viewed inside
W
.

Lemma 1.29
.

The filtration
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}
on
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
also induces a filtration on
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
, so that
the following natural short exact sequence respects the filtrations, using
\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}
for
E^{+}QH^{*}(Y),

0\longrightarrow E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow 0.

Remark 1.30

(
u
-torsion)
.

There is also a canonical long exact sequence

\cdots\longrightarrow E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\longrightarrow E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\longrightarrow E^{-}HF^{*+1}(H_{\lambda})[-1]\longrightarrow\cdots.

One can define a filtration also for
E^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
, by considering the continuation maps
E^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\to E^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda^{\prime}})
for
\lambda<\lambda^{\prime}
, and the LES respects filtrations.
As explained previously,
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is a torsion-free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module, so
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is torsion-free.
For general slopes,
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
may not be torsion-free, but in many examples it is. Indeed, in the notation from
[
RŽ23
]
,
H^{*}(Y)\cong\oplus H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{\alpha}]
often lies entirely in even degrees; if so, we deduce as in the non-equivariant proof in
[
RŽ23
]
that

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\cong E^{-}HF^{*}(\lambda H)\cong\oplus H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})]\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!],

using that all generators are in even grading; thus
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
is free in this case.

Note that
E^{\infty}
models are always torsion-free, and
E^{+}
models are entirely torsion. In the LES, it is precisely the torsion of

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
which is hit by
E^{+}HF^{*-1}(H_{\lambda})
. Since the direct limit
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is torsion-free, it follows that the direct limit of the LES becomes an SES:
Equation

6
.

The
u
-valuation

\mathrm{val}^{\lambda}(v)=\max\{j\in\mathbb{N}\cup\infty:v\in\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}\}
for
v\in E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
relates the filtrations:

(17)

\begin{split}(v\in\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{\lambda})\Longleftrightarrow(\mathrm{val}^{\lambda}(v)\geq j+1)\Longleftrightarrow E^{+}c_{\lambda}(v\otimes u^{-j})=0\in E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda}).\\
\mathrm{val}^{\lambda}(v)=j\Longleftrightarrow\left(\;E^{+}c_{\lambda}(v\otimes u^{-j})\neq 0\quad\textrm{but}\quad u\cdot E^{+}c_{\lambda}(v\otimes u^{-j})=0\;\right).\end{split}

This is the key idea to the following result.
Let
\mathbb{F}_{j}:=QH^{*}(Y)\otimes u^{-j}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
be the obvious
6
6

6

Via

Lemma

1.4
,
or intrinsically:
\mathbb{F}_{j}=\ker(u^{j+1}:E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}QH^{*+2j+2}(Y))
.
nested
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodules of
E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)
, for
j\geq 0
. Abbreviate
V:=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
.

Theorem 1.31
.

For all
j\geq 0
and
p\in[0,\infty]
, we have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules

\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}/u^{j+1}V\to\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}\cap\mathbb{F}_{j},\;\;v\mapsto[v\otimes u^{-j}],

whose images define an exhaustive nested filtration
\cup_{j\geq 0}(\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}\cap\mathbb{F}_{j})=\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}=\ker E^{+}c_{p^{+}}.

This stabilises to
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}=\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}\cap\mathbb{F}_{j}
when
j\geq\max\{j:u^{j}
is an invariant factor of
E^{-}c_{p^{+}}\}
and
E^{-}c_{p^{+}}
is injective.

A similar statement holds for
\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}(V^{-})/u^{j+1}V^{-}\to\mathscr{E}_{m}^{p}(V^{+})\cap\mathbb{F}_{j-m+1}
,
v\mapsto[v\otimes u^{m-1-j}]
.

1.7.
Simple examples:
\mathbb{C},T^{*}\mathbb{C}P^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}P^{1}}(-1)

Example 1.32
.

Let
Y=\mathbb{C}
with the standard
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action. Let
(a,b)
be free, equivalently
a\notin b\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.

(18)

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\!\!\!E^{-}\!SH^{*}(\mathbb{C})\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!),&&\!\!\!\!\!\!E^{\infty}SH^{*}(\mathbb{C})\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!),&&\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!E^{+}\!SH^{*}(\mathbb{C})=0,\\
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot x_{k},&&E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)\cdot x_{0},&&E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong\mathbb{F}[2k],\end{array}

where
x_{0}
comes from the unit
1\in QH^{0}_{S^{1}}(Y)
, and
x_{k}=\mathcal{S}^{-k}(x_{0})\in E^{-}HF^{-2k}(H_{k^{+}})
where
\mathcal{S}
is a map that we discuss later in
Section

1.8
.

Let
p\in[k,k+1)
, for
k\geq 0\in\mathbb{N}.
Then

\mathrm{val}^{p}(x_{0})=k=\lfloor p\rfloor,

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}=E^{-}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C})=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot x_{0}\textrm{ for }j\leq k,\;\textrm{ and }\;\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot u^{j-k}x_{0}\textrm{ for }j>k.

The filtration polynomial is

f_{p}(t)=t^{k},

and the
slice polynomial is

s_{p}(t)=1+t+\cdots+t^{k}.

Example 1.33
.

Let
Y
be the blow-up of
\mathbb{C}^{2}
at the origin. So
Y
is the total space of
\mathcal{O}(-1)\to\mathbb{C}P^{1}
, and consider the standard
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on fibres.
Let
(a,b)
be a free weight, equivalently
a\notin b\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\hskip-19.37494ptE^{-}\!SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!),&&\hskip 12.91663ptE^{\infty}\!SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{2},&&\hskip 15.0694ptE^{+}\!SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{F},\\
E^{-}\!HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot x_{0}\oplus\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot x_{k},&&E^{\infty}\!HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{2},&&E^{\infty}\!HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong\mathbb{F}\end{array}

where
x_{0}
comes from the unit
1\in QH^{0}_{S^{1}}(Y)
, and
x_{k}=\mathcal{S}^{-k}(x_{0})
in grading
|x_{k}|=-2k
.
More explicitly,

E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot x_{0}\oplus\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)\cdot[\omega+Tx_{0}+u^{\geq 1}\textrm{-corrections}],\;\quad SH^{*}(Y)\cong QH^{*}(Y)/\mathbb{K}[\omega+Tx_{0}]\cong\mathbb{K}\cdot x_{0}.

For
p\in[k,k+1)
, the filtration polynomial is

f_{p}=1+t^{k}
, and the
slice polynomial is
s_{p}=2+t+\cdots+t^{k}.

Example 1.34
.

Let
Y=T^{*}\mathbb{C}P^{1}
(the total space of
\mathcal{O}(-2)\to\mathbb{C}P^{1}
), with the standard
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on fibres. Let
(a,b)
be a free weight, equivalently
a\notin b\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.
Then

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\hskip-55.97205ptE^{-}\!SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{2},&&\hskip 12.91663ptE^{\infty}\!SH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{2},&&\hskip-55.97205ptE^{+}\!SH^{*}(Y)=0,\\
E^{-}\!HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot x_{k}\oplus\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot x_{k-1},&&E^{\infty}\!HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{2},&&E^{\infty}\!HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong\mathbb{F}[2k]\oplus\mathbb{F}[2k-2],\end{array}

where
x_{0}
comes from the unit
1\in QH^{0}_{S^{1}}(Y)
, and
x_{k}=\mathcal{S}^{-k}(x_{0})
has grading
|x_{k}|=-2k
.

The slice and filtration polynomials, and the invariant factors of
c_{k^{+}}
, land in one of two cases:

\begin{split}s_{k}=N_{k}(t)&:=2+2t+\cdots+2t^{k-1}+t^{k}+t^{k+1},\;\;\;\hskip 4.30554ptf_{k}=t^{k-1}+t^{k+1},\;\;\hskip 4.30554pt\textrm{invariant factors }(u^{k-1},u^{k+1});\\
s_{k}=Z_{k}(t)&:=2+2t+\cdots+2t^{k-1}+2t^{k},\qquad\;\;\;\;\;\;\hskip 4.30554ptf_{k}=2t^{k},\hskip 43.05542pt\textrm{invariant factors }(u^{k},u^{k}).\end{split}

For example
s_{0}=Z_{0}=2
, but
s_{1}=N_{1}=2+t+t^{2}
.
We prove that
s_{k}=N_{k}
holds for infinitely many
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
The
N_{k}
case is the interesting one: Floer theory gives rise to a different filtration than the canonical one that measures
u
-divisibility in
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
(which is the filtration that we get in the
Z_{k}
case, up to translating the valuation by
k
).
The
Z_{k}
case occurs if
x_{0}
is more
u
-divisible than expected:
x_{0}
is a multiple of
u^{k}x_{k}
in
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
, so

x_{0}\in\mathscr{F}_{k}^{k}
, equivalently
[x_{0}\otimes u^{-k+1}]=0\in E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
.

The following picture

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{3^{+}})\cong(H^{*}(\mathbb{C}P^{1})\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!])[6]
are in blue. The fat blue dots are the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module generators
x_{2},x_{3}
in grading
-4,-6
. The red circles around pairs of classes means that some
\mathbb{K}

illustrates how subtle the filtration is, by comparing the invariant-factor picture from before, with the natural picture one draws when taking into account the grading.
