\hline\cr F_{j}(V^{\infty})&u^{j-3}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&u^{j-3}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&u^{j-3}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\\
\hline\cr F_{j}(V^{+})&\;u^{j-3}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&\;u^{j-3}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&0\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\;\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|c}\hline\cr&j\leq 0&1\leq j\leq 3&j>3\\
\hline\cr F_{j-1}(W^{-})&\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&u^{j-1}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&u^{j-1}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\\
\hline\cr F_{j}(W^{\infty})&u^{j}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&u^{j}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&u^{j}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\\
\hline\cr F_{j}(W^{+})&u^{j}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]&0&0\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Lemma 4.10
.

uF_{j}(V^{+})=F_{j+1}(V^{+})

for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
(in particular,
F_{j}(V^{+})=u^{j}F_{0}(V^{+})
for
j\geq 0
).

Proof.

The inclusion
\subset
is obvious (and a requirement of filtrations), and equality is obvious when
j\geq 0
. For
j\leq 0
, say
v\in F_{j+1}(V^{+})
, so
u^{|j|}[c_{u}]v=0
.
The
u
-action on
\mathbb{F}
is surjective and so the same holds for
V^{+}
, thus we may write
v=u\widetilde{v}
. Then
u^{|j|+1}[c_{u}]\widetilde{v}=0
, so
\widetilde{v}\in F_{j}(V^{+})
and
v=u\widetilde{v}\in uF_{j}(V^{+})
.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

The
u
-action on
V^{+}
is surjective as
V^{+}\cong\mathbb{F}^{s}
, but there is no
u^{-1}
-action.
However, once an explicit presentation
V\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{s}
is chosen, we can identify
V^{+}\cong\mathbb{F}^{s}
with the vector space
\oplus_{i\leq 0}u^{i}\mathbb{K}
, and then write
u^{-j}\bullet v
to mean that we shift by
-j\leq 0
all powers of
u
in
v\in\oplus_{i\leq 0}u^{i}\mathbb{K}
.
The ambiguity caused by the choice of presentation lies in
\mathbb{F}_{j-1}:=\oplus_{-j<i\leq 0}u^{i}\mathbb{K}
. More invariantly,
\mathbb{F}_{j-1}=\ker u^{j}
, where
u^{j}
acts by multiplication on
V^{+}
.
Then
Lemma

4.10
is more explicitly the statement:

F_{-j}^{\lambda}(V^{+})=(u^{-j}\bullet\ker[(c_{\lambda})_{u}])+\mathbb{F}_{j-1}\qquad(\textrm{for }-j\leq 0).

Lemma 4.12
.

For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
Corollary

4.5
gives rise to the following exact sequence

(56)

0\longrightarrow F_{j-1}(V^{-})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}F_{j}(V^{\infty})\longrightarrow F_{j}(V^{+})\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

The diagram chase can be done abstractly. More concretely: by
Lemma

2.30
we may assume
c
is in Smith normal form. This reduces the problem to noticing the claim holds in
Example

4.9
with
3
replaced by a general
k\geq 0
(and for
c=0:\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
the claim becomes the top row of
Corollary

4.5
).
∎

Remark 4.13
.

The interesting summands labelled by
f_{i\leq a}
in
Theorem

2.38
map in (
56
) by:

0\longrightarrow u^{\phi(j-1-j_{i})}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]f_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}u^{j-j_{i}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]f_{i}\longrightarrow(u^{j-j_{i}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]/u^{1+\phi(j-1-j_{i})}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!])[f_{i}]\longrightarrow 0.

The third module is zero for
j>j_{i}
, and for
j\leq j_{i}
it is
u^{j-j_{i}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
(so
\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{K}}\{u^{j-j_{i}},\ldots,u^{0}\}
as a
\mathbb{K}
-vector space).
The non-interesting summands
f_{i>a}
(spanning
\ker c
) map in (
56
) by (
54
) and (
55
).

4.3.
Relation between the filtrations for
V^{-}
and
V^{+}

Definition 4.14
.

For
v\in V^{-}
, the inclusion
V^{-}\subset V^{\infty}
determines

u^{-j}v\in V^{\infty}
(so
v\otimes u^{-j}
in
V^{-}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)=V^{\infty}
). Denote
[u^{-j}v]\in V^{+}
its image via
V^{\infty}\to V^{+}
.
Note that
V^{+}=\cup_{j\geq 0}[u^{-j}V^{-}]
.

Observe that the
F_{j}(V^{-})
filtration is induced by the following
valuation

\mathrm{val}(v):=\max\{j\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}:c(v)\in W^{-}/T\textrm{ is }u^{j}\textrm{-divisible}\}.

Theorem 4.15
.

For
v\in V^{-}
and
j\geq 0
,

(57)

\begin{split}u^{-j}v\in F_{1}(V^{+})\Longleftrightarrow[c_{u}][u^{-j}v]=0\in W^{+}\Longleftrightarrow(v\in F_{j+1}^{\lambda}(V^{-}))\Longleftrightarrow(\mathrm{val}^{\lambda}(v)\geq j+1),\\
\mathrm{val}^{\lambda}(v)=j\Longleftrightarrow\left(\;[c_{u}][u^{-j}v]\neq 0\;\textrm{ in }W^{+},\;\textrm{ and }\;\;u\cdot[c_{u}][u^{-j}v]=0\;\textrm{ in }W^{+}\;\right).\end{split}

Proof.

We first prove the
\Rightarrow
direction. By
Lemma

4.4
, we have a commutative diagram of exact rows,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{-j}v}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[u^{-j}v]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{-}/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{+}_{\mathbb{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!w^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[c_{u}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}

Note that
v\in V^{-}
maps to
uv=u^{j+1}(u^{-j}v)
via
V^{-}\to V^{\infty}
. So, from the diagram chase, it follows that
c(v)=u^{j+1}w^{-}\in F_{j+1}(W^{-}/T)
. Therefore
v\in F_{j+1}^{\lambda}(V^{-}):=c^{-1}(F_{j+1}(W^{-})).

We now prove the
\Leftarrow
direction. If
v\in F_{j+1}^{\lambda}
then
c(v)=u^{j+1}w^{-}\in W^{-}/T
for some
w^{-}\in W^{-}.

Let
w\in W^{\infty}
denote the image
(u\otimes 1)(w^{-})
of
w^{-}
via
W^{-}\to W^{\infty}
.
By exactness,
w\mapsto 0
via
W^{\infty}\to W^{+}_{\mathbb{F}}
.
The same diagram chase on the right applies: commutativity shows
uc_{u}(v)=c_{u}((u\otimes 1)v)=u^{j+1}w\in W^{\infty}
and therefore
u^{-j}v\mapsto w
in the middle arrow, thus
[u^{-j}v]\in V^{+}
maps to
0
via
[c_{u}]
.
∎

4.4.
Relation between the filtrations for
V^{-},V^{\infty},V^{+}

We continue to assume
V\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{s}
is free, and we now work with directed modules
W_{\lambda}
as in
Section

3.2
together with maps
c_{\lambda}:V\to W_{\lambda}
satisfying
compatibility
:
c_{\mu}=c_{\mu,\lambda}\circ c_{\lambda}:V\to W_{\lambda}\to W_{\mu}
,
for all
\lambda<\mu
. The labelling
\lambda
belongs to an unbounded subset of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
; we call these values the
generic slopes
.

Definition 4.16
.

Define
F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{-}),F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{\infty}),F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{+})
via
Definition

4.8

for the map
c_{\lambda}
. We extend this definition to all slope values
p\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
by intersecting (where it is always understood that
\lambda
is generic):

(58)

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-}):=\bigcap_{\lambda\geq p}F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{-}),\qquad\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{\infty}):=\bigcap_{\lambda\geq p}F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{\infty}),\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}(V^{+}):=\bigcap_{\lambda\geq p}F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{+}).

For
p
generic,
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})=F_{j}^{p}(V^{-})
, etc.
In applications, there is a non-empty interval
(\lambda,\mu)
of generic slopes
\lambda^{\prime}
for which
c_{\lambda^{\prime},\lambda}
is an isomorphism. Write
\lambda^{+}
for any such
\lambda^{\prime}
. Thus,
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})=F_{j}^{p^{+}}(V^{-})
, etc. By compatibility, increasing
p
yields inclusions:
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})
for
p\leq p^{\prime}
, etc.

Define
F_{j}^{\infty}(V^{-}),F_{j}^{\infty}(V^{\infty}),F_{j}^{\infty}(V^{+})
via
Definition

4.8

for the map
c=\varinjlim c_{\lambda}
; equivalently:

(59)

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\infty}(V^{-})=\bigcup_{p}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-}),\qquad\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\infty}(V^{\infty})=\bigcup_{p}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{\infty}),\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad\mathscr{E}_{j}^{\infty}(V^{+})=\bigcup_{p}\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}(V^{+}).

In particular,
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\infty}=\ker[(c)_{u}]=\cup\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}
, where
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\lambda}=\ker[(c_{\lambda})_{u}]
.

There is an obvious submodule
u^{j}V^{-}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})
, since
c_{\lambda}(u^{j}v)=u^{j}c_{\lambda}(v)\in F_{j}(W^{-}_{\lambda}).

Let
T_{\lambda}=
Torsion
(W_{\lambda})
, then the filtration
F_{j}^{p}(V^{-})
is induced by the
valuation

(60)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{val}^{p}(v):=&\max\{j\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}:v\in\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{-})\textrm{ for all }\lambda\geq p\}\\
=&\max\{j\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}:[c_{\lambda}(v)]\in W^{-}_{\lambda}/T_{\lambda}\textrm{ is }u^{j}\textrm{-divisible for all }\lambda\geq p\},\end{split}

The function
\mathrm{val}^{p}
is non-decreasing in
p
, due to the compatibility condition.

Let
u^{j_{i}}(\lambda)
be the invariant factors of
c_{\lambda}
.
By
Remark

4.13
,

F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{+})
has a summand
u^{j-j_{i}(\lambda)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
for
j\leq j_{i}(\lambda)
(so

\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{K}}\{u^{j-j_{i}(\lambda)},\ldots,u^{0}\}
as a
\mathbb{K}
-vector space) and a summand
K_{c_{\lambda}}^{+}=(\ker c_{\lambda})_{u}/\ker c_{\lambda}
as in
Lemma

4.7
. We abbreviate

K_{\lambda}^{+}:=K_{c_{\lambda}}^{+}
and
K_{p}^{+}=\cap_{\lambda\geq p}K_{\lambda}^{+}
. For
j\geq 0
, let

\mathbb{F}_{j}:=\ker(u^{j+1}:V^{+}\to V^{+})\subset V^{+},

so loosely the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodule of elements involving only terms of order
u^{\geq-j}
.

Corollary 4.17
.

For any
j\geq 0,m\in\mathbb{N},p\in[0,\infty]
,
there are isomorphisms of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}(V^{-})/u^{j+1}V^{-}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})\cap\mathbb{F}_{j},\;

\displaystyle v\mapsto[u^{-j}v],

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}(V^{-})/u^{j+1}V^{-}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{E}_{m}^{p}(V^{+})\cap\mathbb{F}_{j-m+1},\;

\displaystyle v\mapsto[u^{m-1-j}v].

If
c_{\lambda}
is injective, then for
p=\lambda

those embeddings are isomorphisms for sufficiently large
j
, namely

j\geq\max\{j_{i}(\lambda):u^{j_{i}(\lambda)}\textrm{ is an invariant factor of }c_{\lambda}\}.

Without assuming
c_{\lambda}
is injective, for
j
as above the map
v\mapsto[u^{-j}v]
yields an isomorphism

\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{\lambda}(V^{-})/(u^{j+1}V^{-}+\ker c_{\lambda})\to\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})/K_{\lambda}^{+}.

Proof.

By
Theorem

4.15
, the map
\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}(V^{-})\to\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})
,
v\mapsto[u^{-j}v]
is a well-defined
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module homomorphism. By
Theorem

2.38
,
\ker c_{\lambda}\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{b}
contributes
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{b}/u^{j}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{b}
to
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})/u^{j}V^{-}
,
and
K_{\lambda}^{+}\cong\mathbb{F}^{b}
to
\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})
. The map
v\mapsto[u^{-j}v]
between them has image
u^{-j}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{b}/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{b}\subset\mathbb{F}^{b}
. If
b\neq 0
(equivalently, if
c_{\lambda}
is not injective), that map is not surjective.
The remainder of the proof will show that the map does however surject onto a complement of
\mathbb{F}^{b}\subset\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})
for
j
as large as in the claim.

By
Remark

4.13
we reduce to the case
c_{\lambda}=u^{j_{i}}:\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
for
j\geq j_{i}
. Thus
23
23

23

The spans are
\mathbb{K}
-vector space presentations. The notation is imprecise for
j_{i}=0
:
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})/u^{j}V^{-}=\{0\}=\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})
.

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})\!=\!u^{j-j_{i}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
,

\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{p}(V^{-})/u^{j+1}V^{-}\!=\!\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{K}}\{u^{j+1-j_{i}}\!,u^{j+1-j_{i}+1}\!,\ldots\!,u^{j}\}\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})\!=\!\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{K}}\{u^{1-j_{i}}\!,\ldots\!,u^{0}\}

(cf.
Example

4.9
for the case
j_{i}=3
). Since the left basis maps precisely to the right basis via the map
v\mapsto[u^{-j}v]
, we get an isomorphism in this case.
If
j<j_{i}
, the basis on the left becomes
\{u^{0},\ldots,u^{j}\}
and it maps to the basis of
\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})\cap\mathbb{F}_{j}=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{K}}\{u^{-j}\!,\ldots\!,u^{0}\}
.

In the statement involving
m
, recall
\mathscr{E}_{m}^{p}(V^{+})=u^{m-1}\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p}(V^{+})
.
So in the case
c_{\lambda}=u^{j_{i}}:\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
we get
\mathscr{E}_{m}^{p}(V^{+})=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{K}}\{u^{m-j_{i}},\ldots,u^{0}\}
. The rest of the argument is then very similar.
∎

Definition 4.18
.

Abbreviate
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}:=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}(V^{-})
and
V=V^{-}
. Define the
\mathbb{K}
-vector spaces

\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}:=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}/u\mathscr{F}_{j-1}^{p}=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}/(\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}\cap uV),\;\;\;\;\;\,\mathscr{P}:=V/uV,\;\;\;\;\;\,\mathrm{gr}_{j}^{p}:=\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}/\mathscr{P}_{j-1}^{p},\;\;\;\,\textrm{ and }\;\;\;\,\mathrm{gr}^{p}\mathscr{P}:=\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{j}^{p},

noting the properties:
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}=\mathscr{P}
for
j\leq 0
,
\mathscr{P}_{j+1}^{p}\subset\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}
,
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}\subset\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p^{\prime}}
for
p\leq p^{\prime}
. Declare
\mathscr{P}_{\infty}^{p}:=0
.

Lemma 4.19
.

There is an identification of
\mathbb{K}
-vector spaces,

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}/u^{j}V\cong\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}\oplus\mathscr{P}_{j-1}^{p}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathscr{P}_{1}^{p}.

Thus,
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}/u^{j}V=d_{j}^{p}+\cdots+d_{1}^{p},
where
d_{j}^{p}:=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{P}_{j}^{p}
.

Proof.

Abbreviate
F_{j}:=F_{j}^{\lambda}(V^{-})
and
V=V^{-}
. Recall
F_{0}=V,
so
u^{j}V=u^{j}F_{0}
. As
\mathbb{K}
-vector spaces,

F_{j}/u^{j}V\cong(F_{j}/uF_{j-1})\oplus(uF_{j-1}/u^{2}F_{j-2})\oplus\cdots\oplus(u^{j-1}F_{1}/u^{j}F_{0}).

Since
V
is torsion-free,
F_{j-i}/uF_{j-i-1}\cong u^{i}F_{j-i}/u^{i+1}F_{j-i-1}
via
v\mapsto u^{i}v
. The claim follows.
∎

Those
d_{j}^{p}
are the coefficients of the slice series
s_{p}
for the filtration
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}

on
V
(
Definition

2.45
).
Each
d_{j}^{p}
contains a summand of
b_{p}=\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}\ker c_{\lambda}
by (
43
). We define the reduced slice polynomial
\widetilde{s}_{p}
as in (
44
) by reducing coefficients to
d_{j}^{p}-b_{p}
.
If
c_{\lambda}
is injective, then
b=0
,
s_{p}=\widetilde{s}_{p}
,
K_{p}^{+}=0
.

Corollary 4.20
.

\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})/K_{p}^{+}=\sum(\textrm{coefficients of }\widetilde{s}_{p})-\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}V
.
If
c_{\lambda}
is injective, then:

\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{E}^{p}_{1}(V^{+})=\sum(\textrm{coefficients of }t,t^{2},t^{3},\ldots\textrm{ in the slice polynomial }s_{p}(t)=\widetilde{s}_{p}(t)\textrm{ for }\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}).

Proof.

This follows by
Lemma

4.19
, using the isomorphism in
Corollary

4.17
that holds for large
j
(the proof explains the role of the
K_{p}^{+}\cong\mathbb{F}^{b_{p}}
summand). We also use
d_{0}^{p}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}V/uV=\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}V.

∎

5.
Equivariant Floer theory: the three conventions

5.1.
Background on equivariant Floer cohomology

From now on
\mathbb{K}
will denote the Novikov field. Recall that the
Novikov field
is a field extension of a base field
\mathbb{B}
, whose choice depends on properties of
Y
and on grading requirements.
When
c_{1}(Y)=0
, we use

(61)

\textstyle\mathbb{K}=\{\sum n_{j}T^{a_{j}}:a_{j}\in\mathbb{R},a_{j}\to\infty,n_{j}\in\mathbb{B}\},

where
T
is a formal variable in grading zero. When
Y
is monotone, so
c_{1}(Y)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\cdot[\omega]
, the same Novikov field can be used but
T
will have a non-zero grading
[
Rit16
, Sec.2A]
. In other situations, e.g. the weakly-monotone setup, the Novikov field is more complicated
[
Rit16
, Sec.5B]
: we briefly discuss and use this more complicated field for example in
Remark

A.2
and
Section

A.5
.

Ordinary and Morse cohomologies are always understood to be computed with coefficients in
\mathbb{K}
.
Below,
(Y,\omega,\varphi)
will denote any symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifold (over a convex base) with
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action
\varphi
.

The equivariant parameter will be denoted
u
, and placed in grading
|u|=2
. One can view
\mathbb{K}[u]=H^{*}(\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty})
because we work with
BS^{1}:=S^{\infty}/S^{1}=\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}
.

Let
C^{*}_{\lambda}
be the free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module generated by the
1
-orbits of a chosen Hamiltonian
H_{\lambda}:Y\to\mathbb{R}
of slope
\lambda
at infinity (meaning
H_{\lambda}=\lambda H
at infinity, where
H
is the moment map for the
S^{1}
-action by
\varphi
).
Our discussion applies equally well to the Morse–Bott–Floer setup, in which case we refer to
[
RŽ24
]
for a description of the generators.

The equivariant Floer differential is a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module homomorphism, of the form (
38
), where

\delta_{0}
is the non-equivariant Floer differential, and
(\delta_{i})_{i\geq 1}
are equivariant corrections (cf. various implementations of this in the literature such as
[
Sei08
,
BO17
,
MR23
]
). We defer to
Section

5.5
the discussion of the choice of
S^{1}
-action used (i.e. how precisely we choose
S^{1}
to act on
1
-periodic Hamiltonian orbits).

We follow the conventions from
[
LJ20
,
LJ21
]
(but use more general
S^{1}
-actions), which we now briefly overview.
The
\delta_{i}
involve counts of Floer trajectories for which the Hamiltonian
H_{z}
and the almost complex structure
J_{z}
are allowed to depend on the parameter
z\in S^{\infty}
in the Borel construction
(S^{\infty}\times Y)/S^{1}
. One counts pairs
(v,u)
, as follows. First, we have a Morse trajectory
v:\mathbb{R}\to S^{\infty}
, using the pull-back of standard Morse-Smale data for
\mathbb{C}P^{\infty}
; in particular, the Morse function comes from a Morse function
\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}:=S^{\infty}/S^{1}\to\mathbb{R}
with one critical point of index
2i
for each
i\geq 0
. Second,
u:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to Y
is a solution of Floer’s equation
\partial_{s}u+J_{v(s)}(\partial_{t}u-X_{H_{v(s)}})=0
. Loosely,
\delta_{i}
counts such pairs
(v,u)
which are isolated modulo the naturally induced
S^{1}
-action, for which the asymptotic critical points for
v
involve a Morse index difference of
2i
. At chain level,
C^{*}_{\lambda}
is a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module freely generated by the asymptotics of such solutions
u
, but implicitly it is also keeping track of the asymptotics of such
v
: a factor
u^{i}
indicates that the index
2i
Morse critical point in
\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}
is being considered.

The above
\delta
determines a well-defined differential on the three models discussed in
Section

3.1
,

(62)

W^{-}_{\lambda}:=W_{\lambda}=H(C^{*}_{\lambda}),\qquad\quad W^{\infty}_{\lambda}:=H((C^{*}_{\lambda})_{u})\cong(W_{\lambda})_{u}^{-},\qquad\quad W^{+}_{\lambda}:=H((C^{*}_{\lambda})^{+}).

Equivariant Floer cohomology does not in general carry a product, so the above are just
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules.
In
Section

8
, we explain under what circumstances it carries a product.

5.2.
Background on equivariant quantum cohomology

The equivariant quantum cohomology at the
\mathbb{K}
-vector space level is ordinary equivariant Morse cohomology, and it always carries a product unlike the Floer case.
Again, we refer to
[
LJ20
,
LJ21
]
for a detailed description of these conventions, and just give the loose overview here.

The equivariant Morse model at chain level is freely generated by critical points associated to
S^{1}
-equivariant Morse data on
S^{\infty}\times Y
, working modulo
S^{1}
. The differential counts pairs of Morse trajectories
(v,u)
in
S^{\infty}\times Y
that are isolated modulo the naturally induced
S^{1}
-action. Here, the required equation for
u:\mathbb{R}\to Y
is
\partial_{s}u=-\nabla f_{v(s)}
, where the Morse data
f_{z}
depends on
z\in S^{\infty}
.

This yields a chain complex
C^{*}(Y)[u]
, where
C^{*}(Y)
denotes the Morse complex for
Y
from the above construction. As before, a factor
u^{i}
refers to the index
2i
critical point of
\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}
. There is no need for an algebraic completion in
u
, unlike the Floer context where arbitrarily high powers of
u
can arise in the differential. The motivation to complete in
u
is therefore Floer-theoretical,

C_{\mathrm{eq}}:=C^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!].

Lemma 5.1
.

H(C_{\mathrm{eq}})\cong H^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
as a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module, where
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y):=H((S^{\infty}\times Y)/S^{1})
is the ordinary
S^{1}
-equivariant cohomology defined using the Borel model
(S^{\infty}\times Y)/S^{1}
.

Proof.

The
E^{-}
-equivariant differential (
38
) for equivariant Morse theory
has a well-defined decomposition
d=d\otimes\mathrm{id}
via
C^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]=C^{*}(Y)[u]\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
, because
d
has no contributions from
u^{j}\delta_{j}
for
j>\dim_{\mathbb{C}}Y
for grading reasons.
∎

We obtain three models as in
Section

3.1
,

(63)

V^{-}:=V=H(C_{\mathrm{eq}}),\qquad\quad V^{\infty}:=H((C_{\mathrm{eq}})_{u})\cong V_{u}^{-},\qquad\quad V^{+}:=H(C_{\mathrm{eq}}^{+}).

we will see in
Section

6.1
that
V
is a free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module of finite rank
\mathrm{rk}\,V=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}H^{*}(Y)
, so the freeness assumptions from
Section

4.2
apply.

The equivariant quantum cup product
\star
involves a count of pseudo-holomorphic spheres, involving almost complex structures
J_{z}
that depend on
z\in S^{\infty}
as in the Floer case. One counts the following triples
(v,w,u)
. A Y-shaped triple
v
of half-infinite Morse trajectories in
S^{\infty}
for suitable Morse data, meeting at a common point
v(0)\in S^{\infty}
. (More precisely, we use “

Y

-shaped graphs”, as we work with cohomological conventions). A
J_{v(0)}
-pseudo-holomorphic sphere
w:\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to Y
with three marked points. A Y-shaped triple
u
of half-infinite Morse trajectories in
Y
that reach, at the finite ends, the
w
-images of the three marked points.

When
w
is constant, that count defines the ordinary (Morse construction of) equivariant cup-product,

\bullet\cup_{0}\bullet+u\bullet\cup_{1}\bullet+u^{2}\bullet\cup_{2}\bullet+\cdots:C^{*}(Y)[u]^{\otimes 2}\to C^{*}(Y)[u].

The non-equivariant cup-product
\cup_{0}
arises when the “Y-shaped graph”
v
in
\mathbb{C}{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}:=S^{\infty}/S^{1}
is isolated, in particular it is additive on Morse indices: in self-evident notation,
u^{j_{1}}q_{1}\star_{0}u^{j_{2}}q_{2}=u^{j_{1}+j_{2}}q_{3}
.
When
w
above is a non-constant sphere, it gives rise to quantum corrections to the
\cup_{i}
involving strictly positive powers of the Novikov parameter
T
in
\mathbb{K}
,
\star_{i}:=\cup_{i}+(T^{>0}
-terms
)
. We obtain a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-bilinear product,

(64)

\bullet\star\bullet=\bullet\star_{0}\bullet+u\bullet\star_{1}\bullet+u^{2}\bullet\star_{2}\bullet+\cdots:C_{\mathrm{eq}}^{\otimes 2}\to C_{\mathrm{eq}}.

Specifically, when
v
above involves an asymptote of Morse index that is
2i
higher than expected (so
u^{j_{1}+j_{2}+i}q_{3}
in the previous notation), the triple
(v,w,u)
contributes to
u^{i}\bullet\star_{i}\bullet
. Thus, the
u^{0}
-terms are non-equivariant quantum terms, and the
T^{0}
-terms are equivariant non-quantum terms, so
u^{0},T^{0}
-contributions define the non-equivariant ordinary cup product.

5.3.
The maps from quantum to Floer cohomology

Equivariant quantum cohomology is identifiable with equivariant Floer cohomology for
H_{\lambda_{0}}
whenever the slope
\lambda_{0}>0
is smaller than the non-zero
S^{1}
-periods. Composing with the equivariant Floer continuation map induced by the homotopy from
H_{\lambda}
to
H_{\lambda_{0}}
, yields a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-linear chain map

c^{*}_{\lambda}:C_{\mathrm{eq}}\to C^{*}_{\lambda_{0}}\to C^{*}_{\lambda}.

So we may apply Sections
2.8
,
2.11
, and
4.2
:

c^{*}_{\lambda}:V^{-}\to W^{-}_{\lambda},\qquad(c^{*}_{\lambda})_{u}:V^{\infty}_{u}\to(W^{\infty}_{\lambda})_{u},\qquad[(c^{*}_{\lambda})_{u}]:V^{+}\to W^{+}_{\lambda}.

Taking the direct limit over equivariant Floer continuation maps, as
\lambda\to\infty
, yields

c^{*}:V^{-}\to W^{-},\qquad c^{*}_{u}:V^{\infty}_{u}\to W^{\infty}_{u},\qquad[c^{*}_{u}]:V^{+}\to W^{+}.

The
\infty
-version is often called the
periodic complex/cohomology
.
We often use the explicit notation:

(65)

\begin{array}[]{rclcrclcrcl}V^{-}&=&E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)&&V^{\infty}&=&E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)&&V^{+}&=&E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\\[2.84526pt]
W^{-}_{\lambda}&=&E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})&&W^{\infty}_{\lambda}&=&E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})&&W^{+}_{\lambda}&=&E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\\[2.84526pt]
W^{-}&=&E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)&&W^{\infty}&=&E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)&&W^{+}&=&E^{+}SH^{*}(Y).\end{array}

These fit into exact sequences as explained in
Section

3.2
.
We will see in
Section

6.4
that
W^{-}
and
W^{\infty}
are usually non-zero due to injective maps from
V^{-},V^{\infty}
, whereas
W^{+}
often vanishes.

Corollary 5.2
.

There is a commutative diagram, for each slope
\lambda>0
, whose horizontal lines are short exact sequences of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)[-2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})[-2]/\textrm{Torsion}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)[-2]/\textrm{Torsion}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{+}_{\mathbb{F}\mathrm{lim}}SH^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{\lambda})_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[(c_{\lambda})_{u}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes\mathrm{id}}

where
E^{+}_{\mathbb{F}\mathrm{lim}}SH^{*}(Y):=\varinjlim E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{F}}
.
Any
\mathbb{K}
-vector space complement to
E^{+}_{\mathbb{F}\mathrm{lim}}SH^{*}(Y)
inside
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
maps isomorphically onto the torsion of
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)[-1]
via the connecting map in (
49
). We will show later, in
Theorem

6.25
, that this torsion vanishes.

Proof.

This follows by
Corollary

4.5
and
Corollary

3.5
.
∎

5.4.
A remark about the telescope versus the exhaustive model

We always take the direct limit over continuation maps at the cohomology level, not at the cochain level. We call this the
exhaustive model
. So
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
,
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
, and
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
always refer to that approach.
The motivation for the name is that equivariant Morse–Bott–Floer spectral sequences (see
[
RŽ24
]
, this involves a consistent construction of a cofinal sequence of
H_{\lambda}
) arise from an exhaustive filtration.

There is a standard construction at cochain level to implement the direct limit over continuation maps for a sequence of Hamiltonians
H_{\lambda}
as we increase the slopes
\lambda\to\infty
, e.g. see
[
RS17
, Sec.4.2]
or
[
Zha19
, Sec.7]
.
This is called the
telescope model
. The two models behave rather differently:

(1)

The equivariant Morse–Bott–Floer spectral sequence converges for the exhaustive model, but may fail to do so for the telescope model (to implement the completed tensor product correctly, one would need to allow suitable infinite sums of elements from different columns).

(2)

For the telescope model, there is a spectral sequence from non-equivariant to equivariant symplectic cohomology, for general homological reasons
[
Zha19
, Cor.2.4]
. So the vanishing of
SH^{*}(Y)
implies the vanishing of all versions of telescope equivariant symplectic cohomology.
The same holds for
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
by
(
51
), see
Corollary

6.8
. However, it can fail for
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
and
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
, e.g. for
Y=\mathbb{C}
. By
[
RŽ23
]
,
SH^{*}(Y)=0
for any symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifold with
c_{1}(Y)=0
, so the exhaustive
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0
vanishes, as well as all telescope models
E^{\diamondsuit}_{\textrm{telescope}}SH^{*}(Y)=0
; whereas
c^{*}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
and
c^{*}:E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
are injective and therefore non-zero.

5.5.
The choice of
S^{1}
-action, and
(S^{1}\times S^{1})
-equivariant Floer theory

There are two
S^{1}
-actions in play in Floer theory on
Y
, acting on
1
-periodic Hamiltonian orbits
x=x(t):S^{1}\to Y
. The
\varphi
-action
induced from the
\varphi
action on
Y
, and the
loop-action
induced reparametrising the domain of
x
,

(w\cdot x)(t)=\varphi_{w}(x(t)),\quad\textrm{ and }\quad(w\cdot x)(t)=x(t+\theta),

where
w=e^{2\pi i\theta}\in S^{1}\subset\mathbb{C}^{*}.

One can therefore define
(S^{1}\times S^{1})
-equivariant complexes, with one equivariant parameter in degree two assigned to each of the two respective actions.
This enlarges the complex, but has the advantage
that one does not need to keep track of changes in the weights
(a,b)
in
Section

1.8
.
In this paper, we are instead following the approach from Liebenschutz-Jones
[
LJ20
,
LJ21
]
: we pick an
S^{1}
-subgroup of
S^{1}\times S^{1}
, by choosing a
weight

(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. More precise details of the action are shown in
Section

5.6
.
This means we only have one equivariant parameter
u
, at the cost of the weights
(a,b)
changing in
Section

1.8
.

Definition 5.3
.

We call
(a,b)
a
free weight
if
ma\notin b\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, for any positive weight
m\geq 1\in\mathbb{N}
of the
\varphi
-action. This condition always holds if
b,a
have opposite signs; or if one but not both
a,b
are zero.

The motivation for the above is
Lemma

5.4
, so on Morse-Bott manifolds of non-constant
1
-orbits in
Y
, the combined action does not fix a geometric
S^{1}
-orbit.
That result is desirable due to our general localisation theorem,
Theorem

6.15
. We remark that

(a,b)\textrm{ free }\Rightarrow(a-b,b)\textrm{ free}.\quad\qquad(a,b)\textrm{ free and }|a|\geq|b|\Rightarrow(a+b,b)\textrm{ free}.

5.6.
Technical Remarks about the choice of Hamiltonians and almost complex structures

For the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action
\varphi
on
Y
, we simplify the notation
\varphi_{e^{2\pi i\theta}}
to
\varphi_{\theta}
, using the parameter
\theta\in S^{1}:=[0,1]/(0\sim 1)
.
We also parametrise the domain of loops by
t\in[0,1]/(0\sim 1)
.
We mimic the conventions in
[
LJ20
, Sec.4.3.1, Sec.5.1]
.
For a weight
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, we define the
S^{1}
-action on
x=x(t)\in\mathcal{L}Y
by

(66)

\theta\cdot_{(a,b)}x:t\mapsto\varphi_{a\theta}(x(t-b\theta)).

The Borel model for Floer theory involves pairs
(w,x)\in S^{\infty}\times\mathcal{L}Y
identifying

(67)

(\theta\cdot w,x)\sim(w,\theta\cdot_{(a,b)}x),

where
\theta\cdot w
denotes a choice of free
S^{1}
-action on
S^{\infty}
.
We assume the reader is familiar with the meaning of equivariant Floer data
H^{\mathrm{eq}}_{w,t}(y)
,
J^{\mathrm{eq}}_{w,t}
in the sense of Liebenschutz-Jones
[
LJ20
, Sec.4.3]
.
In our case, equivariant Hamiltonians and almost complex structures satisfy the following,
24
24

24

To clarify,
\theta
acts on
S^{1}
by
\theta\cdot t:=t+b\theta
, which is consistent with (
66
) because we define
\theta\cdot x=\varphi_{a\theta}\circ x\circ\theta^{-1}
.
for
y\in Y
,

(68)

H^{\mathrm{eq}}_{w,t}(y)=H^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\theta^{-1}\cdot w,t+b\theta}(\varphi_{a\theta}(y)),\qquad\qquad J^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\theta^{-1}\cdot w,t+b\theta}=d\varphi_{a\theta}\circ J^{\mathrm{eq}}_{w,t}\circ d\varphi_{a\theta}^{-1}.

At infinity the Hamiltonian is linear of slope
\lambda
, so

H^{\mathrm{eq}}_{w,t}(y)=\lambda H(y)
when
H(y)
is large. When we write the equivariant Floer chain complex, we use the abbreviated notation
H_{\lambda},I
rather than
H^{\mathrm{eq}}_{w,t},J^{\mathrm{eq}}_{w,t}
.

The Floer action functional is
\mathcal{A}(x,\overline{x})=-\int\overline{x}^{*}\omega+\int H_{w,t}^{\mathrm{eq}}(x(t))\,dt
, for
x\in\mathcal{L}Y=C^{\infty}(S^{1},Y)
, suitably defined on a cover of the free loop space
\mathcal{L}Y
, involving cappings
\overline{x}:\mathbb{D}\to Y
of
x
for contractible loops, or choosing a homotopy
\overline{x}
of maps
S^{1}\to Y
towards a chosen reference loop in the free homotopy class
[x]\in[S^{1},Y]
; we omit these standard details.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
x_{0}
be any
\mathbb{Z}/m
-torsion point of
\varphi
that is not a fixed point of
\varphi
. Consider the Hamiltonian flow of
\lambda H
on the slice
H=H(x_{0})
, for the slope
\lambda=\tfrac{k}{m}
,
k\geq 1\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
x
be the Hamiltonian
1
-orbit with initial point
x_{0}
.
Then:

(1)

a=\lambda b\Longleftrightarrow x
is a fixed point of the action (
66
).

(2)

The only fixed points of (
66
) are the constant orbits and any orbits arising
with slope
\lambda=\tfrac{a}{b}
.

(3)

If
(a,b)
is a free weight, then (
66
) has no fixed points
except for constant orbits in
\mathfrak{F}=\mathrm{Fix}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

By assumption,
x(t)=\varphi_{kt/m}(x_{0})
.
By definition,
\theta
acts by
(\theta\cdot_{(a,b)}x)(t)=\varphi_{a\theta+k(t-b\theta)/m}x_{0}.

As
x_{0}
is not fixed by
\varphi
, the equation
\theta\cdot x=x
is equivalent to:

a\theta+\tfrac{k}{m}(t-b\theta)=N+\tfrac{kt}{m}\qquad\textrm{ for some }N\in\mathbb{Z},

that is:
(a-\tfrac{bk}{m})\theta=N
. As this holds for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
, it forces
N=0
, so
a=\tfrac{bk}{m}
. The claims follow.
∎

6.
Structural results for equivariant quantum and Floer cohomology

6.1.
Equivariant quantum cohomology is free

Proposition 6.1

(Equivariant formality)
.

There are non-canonical
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module isomorphisms

V^{-}\cong H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!],\quad V^{\infty}\cong H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!),\quad V^{+}\cong H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{F}.

Proof.

First we recall a general result due to Kirwan
[
Kir84
, Prop.5.8]
. Given any Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-action on a closed symplectic manifold
M
(it also generalises to Hamiltonian actions by compact connected Lie groups), and working over rational coefficients, there is a non-canonical isomorphism
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(M)\cong H^{*}(M)\otimes H^{*}(BS^{1})
, where
H^{*}(BS^{1})\cong\mathbb{Q}[u]
. The same holds over
\mathbb{K}
coefficients, since we assume
\mathbb{K}
has characteristic zero.
Kirwan’s result may not always hold in non-compact settings
[
Kir84
, Sec.9]
. However, in our setting, we may assume that the moment map is proper and bounded below (see
[
RŽ23
]
), which ensures the ordinary Morse inequalities which are needed in Kirwan’s proof (which does not otherwise rely on compactness).

It follows that the equivariant differential
d
on
C^{*}(Y)[u]=C^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}[u]
is chain homotopic to the ordinary differential
\delta_{0}\otimes\mathrm{id}
. Say
d-\delta_{0}=K\circ d+d\circ K
, where
K
is a chain homotopy for the complex
C^{*}(Y)[u]
.
Then on
C^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]=C^{*}(Y)[u]\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
we have
d-\delta_{0}=K\circ d+d\circ K
(omitting
\cdots\otimes\mathrm{id}
factors in the notation).
This implies not just
V^{-}\cong H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
, but also the analogous results for
V^{\infty}
and
V^{+}
, because that chain homotopy equation remains well-defined if we tensor the coefficient system as needed in (
45
) to obtain the chain complexes for
V^{\infty},V^{+}
.
∎

Consider the spectral sequence induced by the
u
-adic filtration on the Morse-Bott chain complex for
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y)
over
\mathbb{Q}
, arising from the differential
d=\delta_{0}+u\delta_{1}+\cdots+u^{n}\delta_{n}
where
n=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}Y
(cf.
Lemma

5.1
).

Corollary 6.2
.

The
u
-adic spectral sequence for
QH^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y)
over
\mathbb{Q}
converges on the
E_{1}
-page, yielding an isomorphism
H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}[u]\cong QH^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y)
. This induces isomorphisms as in
Proposition

6.1
, by passing to
\mathbb{K}
coefficients.
A basis for
H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})
gives rise to a basis for
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
which at chain level involves only coefficients from the base field
\mathbb{B}
, so not involving the formal Novikov variable
T
of
\mathbb{K}
, and whose
u^{0}
-part is the same at the chain level as for the basis of
H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})
.
In particular, the unit
x_{0}
of the ring
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
is represented at chain level by the unique minimum of a suitable auxiliary Morse function on the Morse-Bott manifold
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}:=\min H
(a component of the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-fixed locus
[
RŽ23
]
).

Proof.

The
E_{1}
-page of the spectral sequence associated to the
u
-adic filtration is
H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}[u]
, as
\delta_{0}
is the non-equivariant differential computing
H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})
.
Consider the dimensions of the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
QH^{*}_{S^{1}}(Y)
, starting from degree
0
and inductively moving up. By Kirwan’s result in the proof of
Proposition

6.1
, these dimensions match up with those for
H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}[u]
, therefore there can be no non-trivial edge differentials from the
E_{1}
-page onwards.
For the final claim: under the isomorphism in the claim, a classical cycle
x
may get mapped to
x+u^{\geq 1}
-terms. This map is grading preserving, so for the minimum
x_{0}\in H^{0}(Y)
there are no
u^{\geq 1}
-corrections for grading reasons.
∎

6.2.
Specialisation from equivariant to non-equivariant

Lemma 6.3
.

There are specialisation maps
\mathrm{ev}_{0}
that fit into a commutative diagram of
\mathbb{K}
-linear maps

\textstyle{E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E^{-}c_{\lambda}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\textrm{ev}_{0}}
\textstyle{E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textrm{ev}_{0}}
\textstyle{QH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)/uE^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\lambda}^{*}}
\textstyle{HF^{*}(H_{\lambda}),}

in particular
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to QH^{*}(Y)
is surjective and corresponds to quotienting by
uE^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
.

Proof.

This follows by
Lemma

3.9
, using
Proposition

6.1
.
∎

6.3.
The family of filtrations, and the slice polynomial

Definition 6.4
.

Abbreviate

QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y):=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
to emphasize that the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
depends on the
\varphi^{a}
-action on
Y
, but not on
b
(the loop-action is trivial on constant
1
-orbits).

Abbreviate
E_{\lambda}^{-}:=E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
,
T_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Torsion}(E_{\lambda}^{-})
, and
c_{\lambda}:=E^{-}c_{\lambda}^{*}
.

Abbreviate by
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}
the filtration on

QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)
induced by
c_{\lambda}:QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)\to E_{\lambda}^{-}
, so explicitly:

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}:=c_{\lambda}^{-1}(F_{j}(E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda}))),\textrm{ where }F_{j}(E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda}))\textrm{ is the canonical }u\textrm{-filtration}.

Above it is understood that
\lambda>0
is generic, whereas for general
p\in\mathbb{R}
we let
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{p}:=\cap_{\lambda>p}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}.

Theorem 6.5
.

QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)
is finitely
u
-filtered by
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}
(
Definition

2.39
), which satisfies

(69)

\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}=\ker\left(QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c_{\lambda}}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{-}_{\lambda}/(u^{j}E^{-}_{\lambda}+T_{\lambda})\right),\quad\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\lambda}=QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y),\quad\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{\lambda}=\ker c_{\lambda},

u\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{\lambda}=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}\cap uQH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}
, and
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}\subset\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda^{\prime}}
for
\lambda\leq\lambda^{\prime}
.

This yields a
\lambda
-valuation

\mathrm{val}^{\lambda}(v)
on
QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)
satisfying (
17
), and we deduce
Theorem

1.31
.

We obtain finite invariants
0\leq d_{j}^{\lambda}\leq a:=\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}QH^{*}_{\varphi^{a}}(Y)=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{K})
for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

d_{j}^{\lambda}:=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}/u\mathscr{F}_{j+1}^{\lambda},

yielding a
slice series

s_{\lambda}(t)
(which becomes a polynomial when
c_{\lambda}
is injective),

s_{\lambda}(t):=\sum_{j\geq 0}d_{j}^{\lambda}t^{j}.

Moreover, ordinary cohomology
P:=H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{K})
is filtered by

F_{j}^{\lambda}(P):=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}/u\mathscr{F}_{j-1}^{\lambda},\quad F_{\infty}^{\lambda}(P):=0,

with associated Hilbert-Poincaré series
f_{\lambda}(t)
whose coefficients are
f_{j}^{\lambda}=d_{j}^{\lambda}-d_{j+1}^{\lambda}
.

Proof.

In the notation from
Definition

2.39
,
P:=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)/uE^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\cong H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{K})
(using
Theorem

6.15
) is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{K}
-vector space, so
Proposition

2.42
implies
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
is finitely
u
-filtered by
\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}
.
The claim about the valuation and
Theorem

1.31
follow from
Theorem

4.15
and
Corollary

4.17
.
The properties of
s_{\lambda}
follow from
Theorem

2.47
.
The final claim follows by

Proposition

2.44
(where
T=0
by
Proposition

6.1
, so
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}P=\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}\,E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
).
∎

Following
Section

4.1
,
we have filtrations on
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
,
E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
and
(E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda}))_{\mathbb{F}}
, which induce filtrations on
E^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)
respectively for
\diamondsuit=-,\infty,+
, by
Definition

4.8
.
Denote these

\quad\mathscr{F}_{j,(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit,\lambda}\subset E^{\diamondsuit}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\quad\textrm{(often abbreviated }\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\diamondsuit,\lambda},\textrm{ also abbreviate }\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\lambda}:=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{-,\lambda}\textrm{ and }\mathscr{E}_{j}^{\lambda}:=\mathscr{F}_{j}^{+,\lambda}),

where
(a,b)
is the weight. Intersecting over all generic
\lambda\geq p
, we define
\mathscr{F}_{j,(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit,p}
for
p\in[0,\infty]
.

The three models of equivariant quantum cohomology fit into a short exact sequence by
Corollary

5.2
.

Corollary 6.6
.

The short exact sequence for equivariant quantum cohomologies is filtered:

(70)

0\longrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{j-1}^{p}[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{\infty,p}\longrightarrow\mathscr{E}_{j}^{p}\longrightarrow 0,

These filtrations satisfy the relations described in
Section

4.4
.

Proof.

This follows by
Corollary

5.2
and
Lemma

4.12
.
∎

6.4.
Vanishing and non-vanishing theorems

Corollary 6.7

(Non-vanishing result)
.

SH^{*}(Y)\neq 0\Leftrightarrow E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\neq 0
.

This occurs for many monotone examples from
[
Rit14
,
Rit16
]
, such as
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}P^{m}}(-k)
for
k=1,2,\ldots,m
.

Proof.

This follows by
Corollary

3.10
.
∎

Corollary 6.8

(Vanishing result)
.

If
SH^{*}(Y)=0
(e.g. this holds if
c_{1}(Y)=0
), then

E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0\;\;\;\;\textrm{ and }\;\;\;\;E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y).

In particular
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is a torsion-free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module, as
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
is a
\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)
-module.

Proof.

This follows by
Lemma

3.8
and
Corollary

3.5
.
If
c_{1}(Y)=0
, this can be proved more directly as in
[
RŽ23
]
: the Hamiltonian
kH
has
1
-orbits of arbitrarily negative grading for large
k
. As the grading of
\mathbb{F}
is bounded above by
0
, it follows that
E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is supported in arbitrarily negative degrees for large
k
, in particular it vanishes in the direct limit as
k\to\infty.

A third proof (although theoretically related to the second) is to use
Corollary

7.1
:

E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{N^{+}})
lies in the same degrees as
E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)[2N\mu]
, but the latter lies in degrees
\leq-2N\mu+\dim_{\mathbb{R}}Y
.
∎

Corollary 6.9
.

Assume
c_{1}(Y)=0
.
Given
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, for sufficiently large slope
k
we have

E^{+}HF^{m}(H_{k^{+}})=0\quad\textrm{ and }\quad E^{-}HF^{m-2}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E^{\infty}HF^{m}(H_{k^{+}})\qquad\textrm{ for all }m\geq q,

in particular
E^{-}HF^{\geq q-2}(H_{k^{+}})
is torsion-free.

Remark.

We will see later that

E^{\infty}HF^{m}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E^{\infty}SH^{m}(Y)
whenever
k>\tfrac{a}{b}
. If
(a,b)
is a free weight (
Definition

5.3
) then
E^{\infty}HF^{m}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}QH^{m}(Y)
.

Proof.

By the second proof of
Corollary

6.8
,

E^{+}HF^{\geq q}(H_{k^{+}})=0
for all sufficiently large
k
(as
q
is fixed).
The rest follows by the long exact sequence, see
Lemma

3.2
.
The Remark will be a consequence of
Proposition

6.11
and

Proposition

6.12
.
∎

6.5.
The weak localisation theorem

Lemma 6.10
.

E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)_{u}\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
, indeed the
u
-localisation of
c^{*}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is canonically identifiable with
c^{*}:E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
.

If
c_{1}(Y)=0
, then
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)_{u}\cong E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
.

Proof.

Localising at
u
makes the last vertical line in
Corollary

5.2
vanish, using the following: localisation commutes with colimits since it is a left adjoint;
E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
is
u
-torsion so
E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})_{u}=0
; thus
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)_{u}=0
and so its submodule
\varinjlim(E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{F}})_{u}
also vanishes.

The final claim follows from this, by combining with
Corollary

6.8
.
∎

6.6.
The localisation theorem

Recall
(a,b)
is the weight from
Section

5.6
.

Proposition 6.11
.

Consider the
E_{1}
-page of the spectral sequence for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
.
The zeroth column is
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
.
Any higher column arising with
S^{1}
-period
p\neq\tfrac{a}{b}
must vanish.
In particular,
E^{\infty}HF^{m}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E^{\infty}SH^{m}(Y)
whenever
k>\tfrac{a}{b}
.

Proof.

We first recall the Borel localisation theorem: for any
S^{1}
-action on a smooth compact manifold
B
, the inclusion of the fixed locus
\mathrm{Fix}\hookrightarrow B
induces an isomorphism:

\textrm{modulo }H^{*}_{S^{1}}(\mathrm{pt})\textrm{-torsion we have }\;\;H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B)\cong H^{*}_{S^{1}}(\mathrm{Fix})\cong H^{*}(\mathrm{Fix})\otimes H^{*}_{S^{1}}(\mathrm{pt}).

Identify
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(\mathrm{pt})=\mathbb{K}[u]
, and call
\mathbb{K}(u)
its
u
-localisation. Then the above becomes

H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}(u)\cong H^{*}(\mathrm{Fix})\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}(u),

i.e. the
u
-torsion in
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B)
gets killed. The
u
-completion is

H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)\cong H^{*}(\mathrm{Fix})\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)
.

We now apply the Borel localisation theorem to the action (
66
) on any (closed) Morse–Bott–Floer manifold
B_{p,\beta}
of non-constant
S^{1}
-orbits in
Y
of period
p=c^{\prime}(H(B_{p,\beta}))
(see
[
RŽ23
]
for a detailed discussion of these manifolds).
By the assumption on the slope
p
,
Lemma

5.4
implies that this action has an empty fixed locus. Thus

H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[u]}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)=0.

In the remainder of the proof, we will be referring to two tricks:

Trick 1.

u
-localisation is a flat functor, so the
u
-localisation of a cohomology module is the cohomology of the
u
-localised cochain complex.

Trick 2.

u
-localisation commutes with direct limits, since
u
-localisation is a left adjoint, and left adjoint functors always commute with colimits.

By Trick 2,
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)_{u}\cong\varinjlim E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})_{u}
. Observe that the equivariant Morse–Bott–Floer spectral sequence converging to
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
collapses at some finite page number since only finitely many columns of the spectral sequence are non-zero – notice that because we reduced the problem to
H_{\lambda}
, we have an upper bound
p<\lambda
on the
S^{1}
-periods
p
for the Morse-Bott submanifolds
B_{p,\beta}
of Hamiltonian
1
-orbits that are involved in the chain complex
E^{-}CF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
.
As each page is the cohomology of the previous page, we may apply Trick 1 finitely many times to deduce that we can
u
-localise the
E_{1}
-page to obtain a spectral sequence converging to
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})_{u}
.

As explained in
[
RŽ24
]
, each column of the
E_{1}
-page arises from a so-called energy spectral sequence starting with the (equivariant) cohomology of the Morse–Bott-manifolds of
1
-orbits
B_{p,\beta}
relevant to the given column. In our setup, that means it starts with the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})
(there is also a degree shift, but we omit this for simplicity, as it does not affect the argument).
That energy spectral sequence
converges on a finite page number: this is essentially a consequence of the fact that
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module, but it also involves an observation arising from Gromov compactness. The detailed proof in the non-equivariant case was carried out in
[
RŽ24
, Prop.B.6]
, and the same argument applies here.
By applying Trick
1
finitely many times, we deduce that we may
u
-localise
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})
. By the Borel localisation argument above,
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})_{u}=0
for all non-zero slopes
p\neq\tfrac{a}{b}
.

The final statement follows because the spectral sequence for
E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is the one for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
after omitting the columns with slopes
p>k
.
If
k>\tfrac{a}{b}
then we just saw that those new columns vanish on the
E_{1}
-page, so that omission causes no change.
∎

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
(a,b)
be a free weight (
Definition

5.3
).
All columns in the
E_{1}
-page of the spectral sequence for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
vanish except the zeroth column,
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
.

Thus we have an isomorphism

c^{*}:E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y).

Proof.

By
Lemma

5.4
and
Proposition

6.11
, only the zeroth column in the
E_{1}
-page is non-zero, and it is
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
.
Thus
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
.
∎

Remark 6.13
.

The proof also implies that in the
E_{1}
-page of the spectral sequence for
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
,
each column (except the zeroth,
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
) is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{K}
-vector space.
25
25

25

The
u
-torsion
\mathbb{K}[u]
-module
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})
from the proof is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{K}
-vector space.
Indeed, a Morse-theoretic chain model for
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})
is the free
\mathbb{K}[u]
-module generated by critical points of a Morse function. There are finitely many critical points as
B_{p,\beta}
is compact. Thus,
H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{K}[u]
-module.
Consider a finite generating set.
As the module is
u
-torsion, multiplying generators by powers of
u
yields a finite
\mathbb{K}
-linear spanning set.

However, the
u
-action on the
E_{1}
-page does not usually agree with the
u
-action on the limit of the spectral sequence.

Remark 6.14

(Non-free weights)
.

The case
(a,b)=(0,0)
is not interesting: this corresponds to applying
\cdot\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}E^{\diamondsuit}H^{*}(\mathrm{point})
to the non-equivariant Floer cohomologies. The case
(a,0)
for
a\neq 0
is free.

Consider a non-free
(a,b)\neq(0,0)
, so
b\neq 0
. Let
p=a/b
. Then there are Morse-Bott manifolds
B_{p,\beta}
of
1
-orbits, arising for slope
p
. They consist entirely of fixed points of the action (
66
), by
Lemma

5.4
.
By Borel’s theorem, their
E^{\infty}
-equivariant cohomology is

H^{*}_{S^{1}}(B_{p,\beta})\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)\cong H^{*}(B_{p,\beta})\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!).

These contribute in the column for slope
p
in the
E_{1}
-page for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
. Just like in the non-equivariant case explained in
[
RŽ24
]
, one must actually consider the energy spectral sequence in that column, whose
E_{1}
-page is
\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(B_{p,\beta})(\!(u)\!)
: some of these classes may kill each other due to local Floer trajectories not detected by Morse theory. The surviving classes make up the column for slope
p
of the
E_{1}
-page for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
. These unexpected classes could kill something in the zeroth column (on the relevant page of the spectral sequence admitting an edge differential pointing to the zeroth column); otherwise they survive to the limit of the spectral sequence. A priori, the
\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)
-vector spaces
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
and
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
may therefore differ in dimension, but at most by
\sum_{\beta}\dim_{\mathbb{K}}H^{*}(B_{p,\beta})
.

Theorem 6.15

(Localisation theorem)
.

Let
(a,b)
be a free weight. The following diagram of isomorphisms commutes, where the top row is the
u
-localisation of
E^{-}c^{*}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)_{u}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)_{u}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(E^{-}c^{*})_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\infty}c^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Proof.

The commutative diagram is the first square in
Corollary

4.5
in the notation (
65
).
The left-vertical isomorphism follows by
Proposition

6.1
.
The right-vertical isomorphism follows by
Lemma

6.10
.
The lower-horizontal map is an isomorphism by
Proposition

6.12
.
The claim follows.
∎

6.7.
Injectivity, and periodicity

We emphasize that we do not assume
c_{1}(Y)=0
. Even in the
c_{1}(Y)=0
case (where much is known about the direct limits by
Corollary

6.8
), we will get a torsion-freeness result that is stronger than
Corollary

6.9
.

Theorem 6.16

(Injectivity theorem)
.

Let
(a,b)
be a free weight. The following maps are injective,

c^{*}_{\lambda}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})\quad\textrm{and}\quad c^{*}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}SH^{*}(Y).

Proof.

This follows by
Proposition

2.51
, using
Theorem

6.15
.
∎

Remark 6.17
.

For other weights,

Theorem

6.15
/
Theorem

6.16

hold if
E^{\infty}c^{*}:E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
is an isomorphism.
Indeed
\ker E^{\infty}c^{*}\neq 0\Rightarrow\ker(E^{-}c^{*})_{u}\neq 0\Rightarrow\ker c^{*}\neq 0
in
Theorem

6.16
.

Proposition 6.18

(Periodicity property)
.

Keeping track of the
(a,b)
-weight, for any model
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty,+\}
, abbreviating by
\mu
the Maslov index of the
S^{1}
-action
\varphi
, we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}:E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda+1})\cong E_{(a-b,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})[2\mu].

These isomorphisms are compatible with continuation maps, see (
115
), and with the filtrations.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of
Theorem

A.1
, and
Corollary

7.7
.
∎

In
Section

7
we will revisit the above isomorphisms in greater detail.

Corollary 6.19

(Periodicity property 2)
.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules

(71)

\mathcal{S}^{k}:E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E_{(a-kb,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)[2k\mu],

such that the continuation map
\psi_{k}
from slope
k^{+}
to
(k+1)^{+}
, for weight
(a,b)
, becomes the map

(72)

r_{a-kb,b}:=\mathcal{S}^{k+1}\circ\psi_{k}\circ\mathcal{S}^{-k}:E_{(a-kb,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)[2k\mu]\to E_{(a-(k+1)b,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)[2(k+1)\mu].

This map satisfies
r_{a-kb,b}=\mathcal{S}\circ c_{1^{+}}^{*}
where
c_{1^{+}}^{*}:E_{(a-kb,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)\to E_{(a-kb,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{1^{+}})
.

When
b=0
, the map
r_{a-kb,b}=r_{a,0}=\mathcal{S}\circ\psi_{0}
is independent of
k
.

The
u^{0}
-part of (
72
) is the non-equivariant rotation map
r:QH^{*}(Y)\to QH^{*}(Y)[2\mu]
from Ritter
[
Rit14
]
: it is
quantum multiplication by a class
Q_{\varphi}\in QH^{2\mu}(Y)
described in
[
RŽ23
]
(cf.
Remark

6.20
).

Proof.

Apply
k
times
Proposition

6.18
:

S_{\varphi}^{k}:E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E_{(a-kb,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{0^{+}})[2k\mu],

then use the isomorphism
c^{*}_{0^{+}}:E^{\diamondsuit}_{(a-kb,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\diamondsuit}_{(a-kb,b)}HF^{*}(H_{0^{+}})
(we simplified the notation for
(c^{*}_{0^{+}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\varphi}^{k}
in (
71
)).
Via (
71
), the continuation map
\psi_{k}:E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\to E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{(k+1)^{+}})
becomes the map (
72
).
The compatibility (
115
) implies
\mathcal{S}^{k}\circ\psi_{k}\circ\mathcal{S}^{-k}=c_{1^{+}}^{*}
. The second claim follows. The claim about the case
b=0
is then immediate. The final claim follows by definition of
r

[
Rit14
,
RŽ23
]
, observing that the
u^{0}
-part of an equivariant chain map is the non-equivariant version of the map.
∎

Remark 6.20
.

We note
Q_{\varphi}\in QH^{2\mu}(Y)
, and it satisfies
Q_{\varphi^{k}}=Q_{\varphi}^{k}
.
If
c_{1}(Y)=0
, this class is nilpotent for degree reasons (see
[
Rit14
]
), unlike the maps in (
80
) which are typically injective (
Theorem

6.21
).
We recall that
Q_{\varphi}
was computed in a family of examples in
[
RŽ23
, Proposition 1.32]
: for
Y
Kähler with
c_{1}(Y)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[\omega]
, assuming all non-zero weights of
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}:=\min H
are
1
(equivalently,
\mu=\mathrm{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
),

(73)

Q_{\varphi}=\mathrm{PD}[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]+T^{>0}\textrm{-terms}\in QH^{2\mu}(Y).

Here,
[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]
is a locally finite cycle; its Poincaré dual
\mathrm{PD}[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]
is the Euler class of the normal bundle of
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}\subset Y
.
For example,
Q_{\varphi}\neq 0
for all Conical Symplectic Resolutions of weight
1

[
RŽ23
, Ex.1.33]
.

Theorem 6.21

(Injectivity theorem 2)
.

Recall
(a,b)
is the weight.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
be a generic slope with
k<\lambda
.
The following continuation maps are injective:
26
26

26

When
b=0
,
a\neq 0
, the weight
(a,0)
is free, and all the maps in (1)–(3) are injective, for all
k,\lambda
. When
(a,b)=(0,0)
no claim is being made: the equivariant theory becomes the non-equivariant theory up to tensoring with
\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}E^{\diamondsuit}H^{*}(\mathrm{point})
.

(1)

c_{\lambda,k^{+}}^{*}:E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\hookrightarrow E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
provided
\tfrac{a}{b}\notin(k,\lambda)
;

(2)

c^{*}_{\infty,k^{+}}:E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\hookrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)

provided
\tfrac{a}{b}\notin(k,\infty)
,

(3)

c_{\lambda}^{*}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})

provided
\tfrac{a}{b}\notin(0,\lambda)
,

(4)

if
(a,b)
is a free weight, then all three maps above are injective.

(5)

if
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{p^{+}})
is torsion-free, then
(1) and (2) also hold for
k
replaced by
p\in[0,\infty]
.

Proof.

We prove this by applying
Proposition

2.51
to
V=E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
(which is free by
Corollary

6.19
) and
W_{\lambda}=E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
. Then
V_{u}\cong E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k})
(
Lemma

2.36
and (
45
)). The
E_{1}
-page for
E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is obtained from the
E_{1}
-page for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
after discarding columns involving
S^{1}
-periods
p>k^{+}
.
A similar observation applies to
E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
and
p>\lambda
.
Also,
c_{\lambda,k^{+}}
corresponds to the inclusion of columns on
E_{1}
-pages.

Proposition

6.11
implies that all columns with period
p\neq\tfrac{a}{b}
will vanish.
The assumption
\tfrac{a}{b}\notin(k,\lambda)
ensures that those inclusions of
E_{1}
-pages become identity maps after
u
-localisation (and edge differentials on
E_{1}
and higher pages will agree).
Therefore the
u
-localisation of
c_{\lambda,k^{+}}
is an isomorphism.
Thus
Proposition

2.51
applies, and claim (1) follows.
For claim (2) we apply the same argument, but we use
W=E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
.
For claim (3) we use
V=E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
(the zeroth column of the
E_{1}
-page) and
W=E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
.
When
(a,b)
is a free weight, all higher columns vanish under
u
-localisation by
Proposition

6.12
, so no condition on
\tfrac{a}{b}
is needed.
Claim (5) follows by the same proof as (1),(2) since it only used that
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
was free.
∎

6.8.
Torsion-freeness

Theorem 6.22

(Torsion-freeness 1)
.

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is torsion-free for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and we have a SES:

0\longrightarrow E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u\otimes 1}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\longrightarrow E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

The first claim follows from
Corollary

6.19
combined with
Proposition

6.1
.
The SES follows from the LES in
Lemma

3.2
, using that
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is free.
∎

Remark 6.23
.

Note the above result is only for a “full rotation” slope
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We have not been able to exclude the existence of torsion in
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{p^{+}})
for
S^{1}
-periods
0<p<1
. By
Proposition

6.18
, if
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{p^{+}})
had torsion, then it would repeat in
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{(k+p)^{+}})
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and the torsion would map to zero via the continuation
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{(k+p)^{+}})\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{(k+1+p)^{+}})
since it factors through the torsion-free
HF^{*}(H_{(k+1)^{+}})
.
In particular, this torsion lies in
\ker c_{\infty,p^{+}}^{*}
.

Corollary 6.24
.

Let
(a,b)
be a free weight, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. There is a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})[-2]\longrightarrow E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\longrightarrow 0.

So
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})[-2]
is a free submodule of
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
(whereas

E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is always
u
-torsion).

E^{+}c^{*}_{k^{+}}:E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is surjective and induces an isomorphism

(74)

\begin{split}&E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)/\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1},\;\textrm{ where}\\
&\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}:=\ker E^{+}c^{*}_{k^{+}}\cong\mathrm{coker}\left(E^{-}c^{*}_{k^{+}}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\right)[2].\end{split}

Proof.

The first claim follows from
Theorem

6.22
, using
Proposition

6.12
.
The surjectivity of
E^{+}c^{*}
follows by factorising the surjection
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\to E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
through
E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)
.
We can identify
E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)
with the cokernel of
q:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u\otimes 1}}{{\hookrightarrow}}E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
, which can be factorised as a composite of injections:

q:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\;\;E^{-}c^{*}_{k^{+}}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u\otimes 1}}{{\hookrightarrow}}E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y).

Quotienting this sequence of maps by
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)
(and its injective images), yields an injection

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})/E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{coker}\,q[2]\cong E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)[2]
. By the SES in the first claim, the image of that map is precisely
\ker E^{+}c^{*}_{k^{+}}
.
∎

Theorem 6.25

(Torsion-freeness 2)
.

In
Corollary

5.2
,

\mathrm{Torsion}(E^{-}SH^{*}(Y))=0\quad\textrm{ and }\quad E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=E^{+}_{\mathbb{F}\mathrm{lim}}SH^{*}(Y)=\varinjlim E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{F}},

and the LES from

Corollary

3.5
becomes a short exact sequence:

0\longrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u\otimes 1}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow 0.

where
u\otimes 1
is
u
-localisation followed by
u
-multiplication.

Proof.

The torsion-freeness of the direct limit
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
follows from
Theorem

6.22
, because any given torsion class would have a torsion class representative in

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
for sufficiently large
k\in\mathbb{N}
. The rest follows from
27
27

27

more directly,

Lemma

3.2
becomes a SES because the connecting map
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)[-1]
must vanish since
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
is torsion (being a direct limit of torsion modules) whereas
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is free.

Corollary

5.2
.
∎

Corollary 6.26
.

Let
(a,b)
be a free weight. There is a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)[-2]\longrightarrow E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\longrightarrow 0.

So
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)[-2]
is a free submodule of
E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
(whereas

E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
is always
u
-torsion).

E^{+}c^{*}:E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
is surjective and induces an isomorphism

(75)

\begin{split}&E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)/\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1},\;\textrm{ where}\\
&\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}:=\ker E^{+}c^{*}\cong\mathrm{coker}\left(E^{-}c^{*}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\right)[2]\end{split}

Proof.

This follows from
Theorem

6.25
by the same argument used to deduce
Corollary

6.24
from
Theorem

6.22
, by replacing the symbols
HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
by
SH^{*}(Y)
, and
c^{*}_{k^{+}}
by
c^{*}
.
∎

6.9.
Positive equivariant symplectic cohomology

From the quotient complex of
E^{-}CF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
, by quotienting out the subcomplex generated by constant
1
-orbits, and then taking the direct limit in
\lambda
, one obtains
S^{1}
-equivariant positive symplectic cohomology
, denoted
E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
.
This is analogous to the non-equivariant construction carried out in
[
RŽ23
,
RŽ24
]
.
That same quotient complex, via (
45
), defines
E^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
for all
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty,+\}.

The long exact sequence induced by the inclusion of the subcomplex is

(76)

\cdots\to E^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)[-1]\to E^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c^{*}}}{{\to}}E^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\to\cdots

By
Corollary

3.5
, we also obtain the long exact sequence between the three models:

(77)

\cdots\longrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u\otimes 1}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\infty}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\longrightarrow E^{+}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\longrightarrow E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)[-1]\longrightarrow\cdots

We now rephrase
Theorem

6.15
. Recall
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}:=\ker([c_{u}]:E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}SH^{*}(Y))
, see (
59
).

Corollary 6.27
.

Let
(a,b)
be a free weight. Then we have
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module isomorphisms

(78)

E^{\infty}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)=0\quad\textrm{ and }\quad E^{+}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\cong E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)[-1]\quad\textrm{ and }\quad E^{+}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\cong\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}[1].

In particular,
E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
is
u
-torsion.
The LES (
76
) becomes the SES of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules:

(79)

0\to E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c^{*}}}{{\to}}E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\to 0,

which as
\mathbb{K}
-vector spaces
28
28

28

Not as
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules: e.g. in CY examples,
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is torsion-free, whereas
E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
is torsion.
yields a non-canonical isomorphism
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong H^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]\oplus E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
.

If
SH^{*}(Y)=0
(e.g. if
c_{1}(Y)=0
), then
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)=0
,
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
,

E^{+}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\cong E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)[1]\quad\textrm{ and }\quad E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\cong E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)[2].

Proof.

The spectral sequence for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
is obtained by the one for
E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)
by omitting the zeroth column. Therefore
Proposition

6.12
implies
E^{\infty}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)=0
in (
78
). Then (
77
) implies the second equation in (
78
).
Taking
\diamondsuit=+
in (
76
) yields the exact sequence

0\to E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)/\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}\to E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)\to\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}[1]\to 0.

By
Corollary

6.26
the map to
E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)
is surjective, hence the third equation in (
78
) follows.

As
E^{+}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
arises as a direct limit, any class has a representative in some
E^{+}HF^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(H_{k^{+}})
. The latter arises from a chain complex which is a direct sum of copies of
\mathbb{F}
: so already at chain level any element is
u
-nilpotent. By (
78
), also
E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
is
u
-torsion.

The injectivity of
c^{*}
from
Theorem

6.16
implies that (
76
) becomes (
79
).

For the final claim, we combine
Corollary

6.8
and
Theorem

6.15
, then we use (
76
) for
\diamondsuit=+
to compute
E^{+}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
, which in turn computes
E^{-}SH^{*}_{\bm{\boldsymbol{+}}}(Y)
by using the second equation in (
78
).
∎

7.
Properties of the slice series, the invariant factors, and the filtration polynomial

7.1.
Rephrasing the direct limit

Corollary 7.1
.

E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}SH^{*}(Y)=\varinjlim E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
is isomorphic to the direct limit of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules

(80)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{(a,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{(a-b,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)[2\mu]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{(a-2b,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)[4\mu]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{a,b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{a-b,b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{a-2b,b}}

using the maps from (
72
).
Moreover, (
80
) can be identified non-canonically with a direct limit

(81)

H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}u^{-\mu}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}u^{-2\mu}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to\cdots

where these maps are typically not the naive inclusions of
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-submodules (cf.
Remark

7.6
)

When
(a,b)
is a free weight, the maps in (
80
) and (
81
) are injective.

Proof.

We apply
Corollary

6.19
.
Pass to non-equivariant quantum cohomology via
Lemma

6.3
,
\mathrm{ev}_{0}:E_{(a-kb,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)\to QH^{*}(Y)
. We obtain the non-equivariant analogue of (
80
)
from
[
Rit14
,
RŽ23
]
:

(82)

QH^{*}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Q_{\varphi}}}{{\longrightarrow}}QH^{*}(Y)[2\mu]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Q_{\varphi}}}{{\longrightarrow}}QH^{*}(Y)[4\mu]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Q_{\varphi}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\cdots

To get (
81
) from (
80
), we use the non-canonical identification from
Proposition

6.1
,

E_{(a-kb,b)}^{\diamondsuit}QH^{*}(Y)[2k\mu]\cong H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}u^{-k}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\subset H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!).

The final claim follows from
Theorem

6.21
.(4).
∎

Example 7.2
.

For
Y=\mathbb{C}
and a free weight, we will see
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})=\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot[x_{k}]
, with grading
|x_{k}|=-2k
(
Proposition

10.2
).
In
Corollary

7.1
, the continuation
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{0^{+}})\to E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{1^{+}})
is first viewed as a map

E^{-}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C})\to E^{-}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C})[-2]
, and then as a new map
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to u^{-1}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
via

\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot[x_{0}]\to\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot[x_{1}]\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cdot[x_{0}][2]\cong u^{-1}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\subset\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!),

where
u^{-1}\cdot 1
corresponds to a multiple of
[x_{1}]
.
In the
[x_{0}],[x_{1}]
bases,

r_{a,b}=n_{a,b}u:\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
, where
n_{a,b}\in\mathbb{K}^{\times}
(although
r=0
, as
Q_{\varphi}=0
for degree reasons, we know
r_{a,b}
is injective by
Theorem

6.21
). The new map becomes
u^{-1}r_{a,b}=n_{a,b}:\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\to u^{-1}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
, so it is an inclusion up to
\mathbb{K}^{\times}
-rescaling.
The units
n_{a,b}
play a non-trivial role when working over
\mathbb{Z}

[
LJ20
, Thm.8.1]
(cf.
Section

10.3
).

Similarly,
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong u^{-k}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
, and
\varinjlim u^{-k}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)
recovers
E^{-}SH^{*}(\mathbb{C})\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(\mathbb{C})
.

7.2.
The Hamiltonians
k^{+}H

Recall that
E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
depends on a choice of Hamiltonian
H_{k^{+}}
of slope
k^{+}
at infinity. Changes in this choice correspond to unique continuation isomorphisms, which are grading-preserving
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module isomorphisms.
We now choose
k^{+}H
, so the only
1
-orbits are constant orbits at points of the fixed locus
\mathfrak{F}=\sqcup\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
(cf.
[
RŽ23
]
).
In the Morse–Bott–Floer model, the chain level generators are critial points
x_{\alpha,\beta}
of auxiliary Morse functions
f_{\alpha}:\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}\to\mathbb{R}
(cf.
[
RŽ24
]
).

The Seidel isomorphisms (
113
) give an identification

(83)

\mathcal{S}^{k}:E^{-}_{(a,b)}HF^{*}(k^{+}H)\to E^{-}_{(a-kb,b)}HF^{*}(0^{+}H)[2k\mu]\equiv E^{-}_{(a-kb,b)}QH^{*}(Y)[2k\mu],

which at chain level is the identity map shifted in grading by
2k\mu
: the constant orbits
x_{\alpha,\beta}
with their constant caps satisfy
Equation

118
, noting that in the target their grading has been shifted down by
2k\mu
.
Unpacking the notation from
Corollary

7.1
:

r_{a,b}=\mathcal{S}\circ c_{1^{+}}:E^{-}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}_{(a-b,b)}QH^{*}(Y)[2\mu],

where
c_{1^{+}}
is the continuation map
E^{-}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{-}_{(a,b)}HF^{*}(0^{+}H)\to E^{-}_{(a,b)}HF^{*}(1^{+}H)
.

We make two basic observations:

(1)

The Seidel isomorphism (
83
)
is the identity map, shifted in grading, so it does not introduce negative powers of
T,u
.

(2)

The continuation map
c_{k}^{+}:E^{-}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{-}_{(a,b)}HF^{*}(0^{+}H)\to E^{-}_{(a,b)}HF^{*}(k^{+}H)
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
can be constructed using a homotopy
H_{s}=\lambda_{s}H
which is monotone,
29
29

29

\partial_{s}H_{s}=(\partial_{s}\lambda_{s})H\leq 0
since we (may) assume
H\geq 0
and
\partial_{s}\lambda_{s}\leq 0.
so Floer continuation solutions are counted with non-negative powers of
T
.

Definition 7.3
.

The continuation map
c_{k^{+}}:E^{-}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}_{(a,b)}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
, suitably composed with Seidel isomorphisms
\mathcal{S}:=\mathcal{S}_{\varphi}
from (
114
), defines a
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-linear map:

(84)

ER_{k}:=\mathcal{S}^{k}\circ c_{k^{+}}=r_{a-(k-1)b,b}\circ\cdots\circ r_{a,b}:E^{-}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{-}_{(a-kb,b)}QH^{*}(Y)[2k\mu].

We call these
rotation maps
, to distinguish them from the Seidel isomorphisms: they need not be isomorphisms, nor injective in general, since we composed Seidel isomorphisms with continuation maps.

The same definition is used also for the other models
\diamondsuit\in\{-,\infty,+\}
:

ER_{k}^{\diamondsuit}:=\mathcal{S}^{k}\circ E^{\diamondsuit}c_{k^{+}}=r_{a-(k-1)b,b}^{\diamondsuit}\circ\cdots\circ r_{a,b}^{\diamondsuit}:E^{\diamondsuit}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{\diamondsuit}_{(a-kb,b)}QH^{*}(Y)[2k\mu].

Corollary 7.4
.

The following properties hold:

(1)

If
(a,b)
is a free weight, then
ER_{k}
is injective.

(2)

The slice series
s_{k}
for
E^{-}c_{k^{+}}
is the same as the slice series for
ER_{k}
.

(3)

The
u^{0}
-part of
ER_{k}^{\diamondsuit}
is
r^{k}:QH^{*}(Y)\to QH^{*}(Y)[2k\mu]
.

Proof.

(1) follows from
Corollary

7.1
; (2) follows immediately because
ER_{k},E^{-}c_{k^{+}}
differ by applying an isomorphism
\mathcal{S}^{k}
; and (3) follows by
Corollary

6.19
.
∎

Consider bases for
E^{-}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)
as described in
Corollary

6.2
.
We abusively refer to it as a basis
y_{i}
for
H^{*}(Y)
, even though the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module isomorphism
H^{*}(Y)[\![u]\!]\cong E^{-}_{(a,b)}QH^{*}(Y)
at chain level means that the
y_{i}
may have
u^{\geq 1}
-correction terms that depend on
(a,b)
(in the same grading as
y_{i}
). The matrix for
ER_{k}
in such bases cannot involve negative powers of
T,u
.

Remark 7.5

(Calabi-Yau and Montone cases)
.

The
2k\mu
grading often imposes further constraints:

(1)

When
c_{1}(Y)=0
, the matrix for
ER_{k}
has
(i,j)
entry
k_{ij}u^{m}
, where

m=(2k\mu-|y_{i}|+|y_{j}|)/2
is required to be in
\mathbb{N}
(otherwise the entry is zero), and
k_{ij}\in\mathbb{K}
involves only
T^{\geq 0}
-terms. Note that for
c_{1}(Y)=0
the Novikov variable
T
lies in grading
|T|=0
, so
\mathbb{K}
lies in grading zero.

(2)

When
c_{1}(Y)=a[\omega]
for
a>0\in\mathbb{R}
, we place the Novikov variable
T
in grading
|T|=2a
. Abbreviate
t:=T^{1/a},
so
|t|=2
. This time, the
(i,j)
entry is a
\mathbb{B}
-linear combination of the monomials
t^{m},t^{m-1}u,\ldots,u^{m}
where
m=(2k\mu-|y_{i}|+|y_{j}|)/2
, again requiring
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

7.3.
Growth rate of the slice polynomial (free weight case)

Assume
(a,b)
is a free weight.
Then
we are in the setting of
Theorem

6.16
and
Corollary

6.26
, so
Proposition

6.1
yields:

(85)

\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{r}\cong E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\!\hookrightarrow\!E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\!\hookrightarrow\!E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)\!\hookrightarrow\!E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{r},

where
r=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}H^{*}(Y)
.
By
Corollary

2.18
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

(86)

\begin{split}E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})&\cong u^{-j_{1}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\cdots\oplus u^{-j_{r}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!],\\
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)&\cong u^{-j_{1}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\cdots\oplus u^{-j_{r-s}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)^{s},\end{split}

so that
E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{r}
is identified with the submodules
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{r}
up to isomorphism, and where

0\leq j_{1}(k)\leq\cdots\leq j_{r}(k),\quad j_{i}(k)\leq j_{i}(k+1),\quad\lim_{k\to\infty}j_{i}(k)=j_{i}\textrm{ for }i\leq r-s,\quad\lim_{k\to\infty}j_{i}(k)=\infty\textrm{ for }i>r-s.

The invariant factors of the continuation map
c_{k^{+}}:E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\hookrightarrow E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
are
(u^{j_{1}(k)},\ldots,u^{j_{r}(k)})
. These are well-defined invariants as
c_{k^{+}}
is a homomorphism of free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules (cf.
Section

2.1
).

The slice polynomial
s_{k}
determined by the continuation map
c_{k^{+}}
satisfies

s_{k}=\sum d_{j}(k)t^{j},\qquad d_{j}(k)=\#\{i:-j_{i}\leq-j\},\qquad 0\leq d_{j}(k)\leq d_{j}(k+1)\leq r,\qquad\lim_{k\to\infty}d_{j}(k)=d_{j}.

As
k\to\infty
, the
s_{k}
limit to a series
\sum d_{j}t^{j}
with tail end
s(t^{j_{r-s}+1}+t^{j_{r-s}+2}+\cdots)
.
This series is a polynomial precisely when
s=0
, i.e. when
E^{-}SH^{*}(Y)
is a free
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module.

In the notation from
Definition

2.15
and
Lemma

4.19
,

(87)

\begin{split}d_{j}(k)&=\dim_{-j}E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{F}_{j}^{k}/u\mathscr{F}_{j-1}^{k},\\
d_{j}&=\dim_{-j}E^{-}SH^{*}(Y),\qquad(\textrm{in particular }d_{0}=r)\\
s&=\dim_{-\infty}E^{-}SH^{*}(Y),\end{split}

By
Corollary

6.26
,

(88)

\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}=\ker(E^{+}c_{k^{+}}:E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}}))\cong\mathrm{im}(u\otimes 1)/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{r},

where
u\otimes 1:E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\hookrightarrow E^{\infty}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong E^{\infty}QH^{*}(Y)
.

By (
74
),
Corollary

4.20
and
Lemma

2.17
,

(89)

\begin{split}\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}&=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\,E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})/E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\\
&=s_{k}(0)\\
&=d_{1}(k)+d_{2}(k)+\cdots\\
&=j_{1}(k)+j_{2}(k)+\cdots+j_{r}(k).\end{split}

In greater detail, (
74
) yields

(90)

\begin{split}\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}&\cong\left(E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})/E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\right)[2]\\
&\cong u\cdot E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})/u\cdot E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\\
&\cong(u^{1-j_{1}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\cdots\oplus u^{1-j_{r}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!])/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{r},\\
E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})&\cong E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)/\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}\\
&\cong\mathbb{F}^{r}/(u^{1-j_{1}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\cdots\oplus u^{1-j_{r}}(k))\\
&\cong\mathbb{F}[2j_{1}(k)]\oplus\mathbb{F}[2j_{2}(k)]\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{F}[2j_{r}(k)].\end{split}

and more precisely it yields isomorphisms

(91)

\begin{split}\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}&\cong\left(E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})/E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\right)[2]\\
&\cong u\cdot E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})/u\cdot E^{-}QH^{*}(Y)\\
&\cong(u^{1-j_{1}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\cdots\oplus u^{1-j_{r}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!])/u\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]^{r},\\
E^{+}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})&\cong E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)/\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}\\
