&\cong\mathbb{F}^{r}/(u^{1-j_{1}(k)}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\cdots\oplus u^{1-j_{r}}(k))\\
&\cong\mathbb{F}[2j_{1}(k)]\oplus\mathbb{F}[2j_{2}(k)]\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{F}[2j_{r}(k)].\end{split}

Recall
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}=\ker(E^{+}QH^{*}(Y)\to E^{+}SH^{*}(Y))
, so
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}=\lim\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{E}^{k}_{1}=d_{1}+d_{2}+\cdots
. Thus,
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}=\infty
for
s\neq 0
, whereas for
s=0
:

\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{1}=d_{1}+d_{2}+\cdots=j_{1}+j_{2}+\cdots+j_{r}.

Finally, observe that (
86
) and the SES in
Corollary

6.26
imply

E^{+}SH^{*}(Y)\cong E^{\infty}SH^{*}(Y)/(u\otimes 1)(E^{-}SH^{*}(Y))\cong\mathbb{F}[2j_{1}]\oplus\mathbb{F}[2j_{2}]\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{F}[2j_{r-s}],

where the latter is the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
\mathbb{F}^{r-s}
but the
u^{0}\in\mathbb{F}
in the
m
-th coordinate of
\mathbb{F}^{r-s}
arises from
[u^{-j_{m}}]\in\mathbb{K}(\!(u)\!)/u\cdot u^{-j_{m}}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
, so actually lies in grading
-2j_{m}
, yielding the shifted summand
\mathbb{F}[2j_{m}]
.

Remark 7.6
.

E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})\cong H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}u^{-2k\mu}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
as
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-modules (cf.
Corollary

7.1
), but the continuation from
H_{k^{+}}
to
H_{(k+1)^{+}}
may not be an inclusion
H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}u^{-2k\mu}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\hookrightarrow H^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}u^{-2(k+1)\mu}\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
,
so
d_{n}(k)=\dim_{-n}\,E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{k^{+}})
are not just a naive Betti number count.
The following picture is the
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
-module
A:=E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{0^{+}})\cong E^{-}QH^{*}(T^{*}\mathbb{C}P^{1})\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!][-2]
: the fat dots generate
H^{*}(T^{*}\mathbb{C}P^{1})
as a
\mathbb{K}
-vector space, the dots above are their images under the
u
-action, and the dots below are the two new generators needed for
B:=E^{-}HF^{*}(H_{1^{+}})\cong E^{-}QH^{*}(T^{*}\mathbb{C}P^{1})[2]\cong\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!][2]\oplus\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
.
The numbers are the
\mathbb{Z}
-grading. Depending on how
A
injects into
B
, we either get invariant factors
(1,u^{2})
(so
j_{1}=0
,
j_{2}=2
) or
(u,u)
(so
j_{1}=j_{2}=1
).
The
(u,u)
case is the naive summand-preserving inclusion
\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]\oplus\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!][-2]\subset\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!][2]\oplus\mathbb{K}[\![u]\!]
.
We will show that
(1,u^{2})
occurs for all free weights
(a,b)
.
